# Egypt — Maths books

**Egypt is CLOSED in the library, and its maths set is 74 books recorded, of
which 73 are in hand** (all recorded as student books; no teacher guides are in
the Egypt maths record). The 73 held files were read and filed below in six
grade bands on 2026-11-04: **9,168 PDF pages** (file pages, counted with
pdfinfo — see the note on the doubled file below), resolving into **30 distinct
works**. Every band's filing then passed a separate citation check
(checker ≠ reader): **932 anchors checked against the books' own pages — 848
passed, 79 corrected in place, 5 not found**. The per-band counts and every
correction stand in the check block at the end of each band below. Where this
header and a band section disagree, the section wins: the header was
cross-checked against the sections, and the sections carry the per-book
evidence.

**The file count, reconciled plainly:** the library's books.json records 74
Egypt maths entries. 73 were grabbed and held; 1 is owed (see below). The six
band lists (12 + 10 + 10 + 8 + 9 + 24) sum to 73 unique files, no file in two
bands. Parallel language editions of one book are filed as ONE work throughout,
which is why a file count and a distinct-work count differ: 73 files, 30
distinct works. One counting caution: the 9,168 total counts file pages, and
Band F's Arabic General Mathematics Term 1 file holds two concatenated copies
of one book (191 file pages for a ~101–106-page book), so the unique-content
page count is about 90 pages lower than the file-page total.

**The course, in outline:** Egypt's maths runs as term books in the primary
stage (Grades 1–6), Preparatory books (Grades 7–9) and Secondary books
(Grades 10–12). Two primary series overlap in the held set and must not be
merged: the older Education 2.0 "Discover"/"Discovery" workbook series and the
newer SPRIX/CACD series (Point! / Warm Up / How to Solve / Try / Exercise).
Grade 11 splits by section — Science-section pupils take Pure Mathematics and
Applied Mathematics ("Mathematics Applications" on the covers) per term, while
Arts-section pupils take General Mathematics, an abridged Pure book, not a
separate syllabus. Grade 12 has no term split in this set: each Grade 12 subject
is a single full-year volume. Publisher throughout is the Ministry of Education
and Technical Education (Egypt). Languages are Arabic, English and French
throughout the set, but NOT every book in every language — the gaps below give
the exact language coverage, and Grade 11 Term 2 is Arabic/English only in all
three branches while Grade 12 is a full-year volume set with no term split.

**PAGINATION WARNING — read before citing any page:** never cite a PDF page as
a printed page for Egypt maths. Bands E and F proved PDF ≠ printed for nearly
every secondary book, and the offsets differ by book and by language edition
(for example Grade 7 Term 1: PDF = printed + 6 in all three languages; Grade 8
Term 1: + 5; Grade 7/8 Term 2 Arabic: + 4; Grade 9 new Term 1: + 5; Secondary 1
Term 1: English + 4, Arabic + 2, French + 6; Secondary 1 Term 2: English + 5,
Arabic + 2; Grade 5 Term 1 Arabic: + 11, Grade 6 Term 1 Arabic: + 9, Grade 6
Term 2 Arabic: + 7, with French offsets again different). Every unit map below
uses the books' own PRINTED pages, and each work states its verified per-file
offset. Contents-page numbers are also unreliable in places (notably Grade 3
new-series contents running 4–5 below body folios, and Grade 4 French contents
running 2 below its body folios from Unit 5 on) — use the body folios in the
maps, not contents numbers, where a band section says so.

passed/corrected/not found):**
- Band A, Grades 1–2: 12 files → 4 works → 1,142 pages → 100 anchors
  (91 passed, 9 corrected, 0 not found).
- Band B, Grades 3–4: 10 files → 4 works → 1,241 pages → 93 anchors

- Band F, Grades 11–12: 24 files → 9 works → 3,225 pages → 179 anchors

- **Total: 73 files → 30 works → 9,168 pages → 932 anchors (848 passed,

**THE GAPS, PROVEN FROM THE BOOKS (nothing below is inferred from a missing
record row alone — each was checked against the held files):**
- **Grade 2 Term 2:** no file in the held set at all (only Term 1 is held, in
  two different series).
- **Grade 3:** no French Term 2 in any file (the French Term 1 file ends at its
  Chapter 6 capacity reflection); no new-series (SPRIX) Term 2 at all — its
  Term 1 book ends at "Vertical Multiplication (Part 1)" with no Part 2 held;
  the new-series Term 1 is held in Arabic and English only, no French copy.
- **Grade 4:** no Term 2 in any language — all three held files end at Unit 8
  (order of operations) followed by revision credits/back matter.
- **Grade 5 Term 2 and Grade 6 Term 2:** Arabic and English only, no French
  copy of either.
- **Grades 7 and 8 Term 2:** Arabic only — the English and French files are
  Term 1 cover to cover (Final Assessments at printed 112–113 for Grade 7 and
  121–122 for Grade 8), with no Term 2 material hiding in them.
- **Grade 9:** the new (GPS) edition is held as Term 1 in Arabic only — no new
  Term 2, and no English or French copy of the new Term 1; the old edition's
  Term 1 back matter (answers, general exercises, exam models) is missing in
  the held files — the held contents pages list no back matter at all, and
  Term 1 ends mid-practice of lesson 5-4 in all three printings, so the old
  85/86/99 page numbers that once circulated for it must not be counted as held.
- **Grade 10 Term 2:** no French book — the file recorded as French Term 2 is
  the English Term 2 book throughout (cover, title page, contents and body).
- **Grade 11 Term 2:** no French copy in any branch (Pure, Applied or General)
  — the only French files are Grade 11 Term 1 in all three branches plus the
  three full-year Grade 12 books; the Arabic General Term 1 second copy
  repeats Term 1, it is not a hidden Term 2.
- **Grade 12:** no geometry unit in Applied Mathematics Grade 12 — solid
  geometry lives in Applied Grade 11 Term 1 and 3D geometry in Pure Grade 12;
  and one whole file is owed (below).
- **No teacher guides** are in the Egypt maths record at all, for any grade.

**RECORD CORRECTIONS, PROVEN BY COVERS AND IMPRINTS (full detail in each
band's own corrections section):**
1. **Grade 2 Term 1 is two different series, not one book twice.** The
   record's "2026 vs 2026_2027" pairs are the old Education 2.0 "Discover"
   workbook (60 lessons, no vertical algorithms) versus the new SPRIX/CACD
   series (41 taught lessons, vertical algorithms, capacity, duration).
   Conversely, Grade 1 Term 1 English in two printings (2025/2026 publisher
   PDF vs 2026–2027 printed-book scan, ISBN 978-633-8475-39-0) is ONE work —
   same chapters, pagination and lessons.
2. **Grade 3 Term 1 is likewise two different editions, not duplicates:**
   green Discovery 2025–2026 (Chapters 1–6) vs orange SPRIX 2026/2027
   (10 chapters, vertical methods, remainders, no geometry). Grade 4 is one
   work across its record years: the French copy's 2025–2026 dating and the
   English/Arabic copies' 2026–2027 dating are reprint years of the same
   ©2025 Discovery Techbook. Record years elsewhere are school-year shorthand:
   "2026" for the old-series and French Grade 4 books means 2025–2026.
3. **Grade 9 combined files misrecorded as single terms.** All three old-edition
   Grade 9 files (Arabic, English, French) hold BOTH terms — Term 1 followed by
   Term 2, each with its own pagination — not Term 1 or Term 2 alone as the
   record says. Consequence: Grade 9 Term 2 is held in all three languages,
   not Arabic only.
4. **Grade 10 "French" Term 2 file is English** (see gaps above). Grade 10
   Term 1 record years "2026_2027" (Arabic/English) are NOT printed in those
   books — English imprint says Issued 2013 (ISBN 978-977-706-005-9); keep
   2026_2027 only as a source/release year. The French Term 1 record year
   "2026" is the second half of its printed first edition 2025-2026
   (ISBN 978-977-706-009-7).
5. **Grade 10 Term 2 contents over-count lessons.** Lesson 4-5 (listed in
   contents) has no separate lesson in either the Arabic or the English body;
   neither does lesson 3-4 in either body; and in the English body lessons
   4-3 and 4-4 likewise have no separate lesson (the Arabic body does have
   4-3 and 4-4, at PDF pages 109 and 113).
6. **Band F record fields were blank and are now filled from the books:**
   Grade 11 Term 1 in Arabic, English and French for all three branches;
   Grade 11 Term 2 in Arabic and English only; Grade 12 full-year (no term) in
   all three languages for Pure, Applied and Statistics; school year
   2025–2026 for the Grade 11 books and Grade 12 Pure; Statistics held in
   2024–2025 prints (English, Arabic) and 2025/2026 (French) — the French
   Statistics print year is 2025/2026, not 2024–2025; Grade 12 Applied: no year
   on the English copy, 2025-2026 on the French cover, no deposit/ISBN printed
   in either. Grade 12 must not be split into Term 1/Term 2 slots.
7. **French Pure Term 1 imprint must not be trusted:** it prints deposit
   10557/2015, ISBN 978-977-706-014-1 and a reversed year ("2026/2025") —
   those are the Term 2 book's numbers. The file itself is Term 1 (cover
   "Premier Semestre", Term 1 contents); Term 1 deposit/ISBN remain
   unverified. (The same reversed-year print also occurs on the English
   General Term 1 imprint, there with the correct Term 1 deposit/ISBN, so a
   reversed year alone does not prove a wrong book.)
8. **Arabic General Term 1 file is a doubled file, not a longer book:** 191
   PDF pages are two concatenated copies (one complete, one partial ending at
   printed p. 83); the book is ~101–106 pages like its English/French editions.
   Same-work page-count differences elsewhere in Band F (Pure Term 2 Arabic
   130 vs English 136; Pure Grade 12 English 199 vs Arabic/French 203; Applied
   Grade 12 161–163) are scan variance, not different editions.
9. **Smaller cover-proved points:** Grade 8 English record title
   "Mathematics Preparatory 2 Term 1" is inconsistent with its siblings
   ("Preparatory 7/8") — the book says "Second Preparatory", i.e. Grade 8;
   "Mtah" in the Grade 7 English/French filenames is a typo in the ministry
   source filename itself (the blob URLs carry it too); record/filename
   spellings "Application_Mathematics", "applid_math" and "genral_math" are
   misspellings of the printed "Mathematics Applications" and "General
   Mathematics"; the new SPRIX English Grade 3 file's PDF metadata title
   wrongly mentions Grade 2 (the book itself is Grade 3 throughout); Grade 6
   Term 1 Arabic year 2026_2027 is confirmed (back-matter print table
   2026-2027, barcode ISBN matching the English/French edition — a later
   printing of the same edition); Grade 6 Term 2 English year "2026" is
   record-sourced only — the held English copy prints no year, ISBN or
   copyright page, and the year rests on the Arabic edition's 2025–2026 cover
   and the record itself.
10. **Held-copy defects and slips (in the books, not the record):** French
    Grade 1 Term 1 headers print "Les nombers" for "nombres" (contents correct);
    old-series English contents misplace Grade 2 Chapter 3 Lessons 6–7
    (p. 36 / "??" for pp. 35/36; French contents right); Grade 3 old English
    "LENGHTHS" for "LENGTHS"; Grade 3 new English "Orgainzing date in tableS";
    Grade 7/8 English print typos "striaght angle" (printed 82) and "from the
    give ones" (printed 108), both visually confirmed on the printed pages;
    Grade 12 Applied English contents duplicate "3-2" for Power (body correctly
    3-3; French contents number it correctly); contents typos "Expnentail
    Function", "Heigher deivatives", "Applicattions", "caplanar", "Perparing";
    Grade 5 Term 1 English Lesson 7 has an empty QR box and Lesson 8's
    Whiteboard item 7 prints "4.14  3.09 =" with no operator (the Arabic and
    French parallels carry the minus). Full lists stand in each band.

**Band-boundary resolutions (no work double-counted):** the Grade 9 / Grade 10
boundary is clean — Grade 9's five works (old Term 1, old Term 2, new Term 1)
are Preparatory 3 books and Grade 10's two works (old Term 1, old Term 2) are
Secondary 1 books, with no shared book; the three combined Grade 9 files each
hold two works (old Terms 1 and 2) and are counted once as files, twice as
works in the work total only via their two books. The Grade 11 / Grade 12
boundary is likewise clean: Grade 11's six works are term books in three
branches and Grade 12's three works are full-year books in three subjects, with
General Mathematics counted as the Arts abridgement of Pure, not as a separate
syllabus, and Statistics Grade 12 counted separately from the owed
Economics+Statistics file (below), whose relationship to it is unverified.

**Read method, honestly:** every held file was opened, page-counted and
cover/imprint-checked, and covers/imprints govern over the record throughout.
Term 1 primary books were read in full from their text-layer files where a text
layer exists, with parallel editions checked page-for-page or
section-for-section as each band's method note states; image-only books were
read page by page visually (Preparatory Term 2 books) or through contents,
unit openers and sampled lesson pages across every unit (Secondary branch
books), as each band's method note states — non-English editions were matched
to the English contents and spot-checked, not matched page-for-page, except
where a band section says otherwise. Summaries below are in the readers' own
words, with only very short quoted fragments (a heading phrase, a
worked-example line).

**GRAB-ROUTE HONESTY NOTE — what "in hand" means for Egypt:** the secured
GitHub copies of Egypt maths mostly do not exist. Only **12 of the 74 files
were reachable via GitHub (6 from the git tree + 6 oversized release assets,
disjoint sets)**; **61 were fetched from the ministry's own blob store
(elearnningcontent.blob.core.windows.net/elearnningcontent/ — the misspellings
are part of the real paths)**; the remaining 1 is the owed file below, which
failed on every route. The inventory's "in hand" for Egypt therefore means
"recorded in books.json" (and, for the 73 held files, grabbed and read from
the ministry blob or GitHub copies listed in the grab records), NOT "secured
in the git tree". Anyone re-securing Egypt maths must re-grab from the blob
store; the git tree alone will not reproduce this set.

**ONE OWED FILE — counted nowhere, named here and in the gap list:**
grade-12/gov/egypt/Economics_Statistics_3sec.pdf — "Statistics and Economics
Secondary 12 Term 1" (Arabic, 2026, Ministry of Education and Technical
Education, Egypt). Routes tried and failed 2026-11-04: its repo books.json
ministry URL 404s; 7 HEAD-probed URL variants 404; Azure container listing
disabled (404 ResourceNotFound); a Telegram copy exists (sent to
@liqlearns_textbooks 2026-10-13, message_id 2759) but Telethon fetch failed
from this host (proxy blocks Telegram data centres); web search found no exact
2026 file (only a third-party 2025 edition — not the same file).
**Consequence:** whether its statistics part duplicates the Statistics Sec3
books, and what economics content it carries, is UNVERIFIED until the file
lands; any economics content is a real gap until then.

## Band A — Grades 1–2

Reader's note: this is a course-builder's read, not a transcription. Summaries below are
in the reader's own words, with only very short quoted fragments (a heading phrase, a
worked-example line). Maths content is filed in English throughout; each book's own
language is stated. Printed page numbers are used for all lesson maps; the PDF-page
relationship is stated per file and was verified, never assumed.

Band totals: 12 files, **4 distinct works**, **1,142 PDF pages** (see clustering table
at the end). All 12 files opened, passed the `%PDF` check, and were read: the four
works were each read in full — the two new-series Grade 1 works from their text-layer
files, the two Grade 2 works page by page from rendered page images.

Two series are in play in this band, and they must not be confused:

- **New series** (cat "Brainy", dog "Smarty", chicken "Genius"; SPRIX/CACD imprint;
  Japanese editorial team; lesson format Point! / Warm Up / How to Solve / Try /
  Exercise; a numbered side tab strip 1–17 with the current chapter highlighted):
  Grade 1 Terms 1–2 (2025/2026, with a 2026/2027 printing of Term 1) and Grade 2
  Term 1 (2026/2027).
- **Old series** (Education 2.0 "Discover" series, blue school-objects cover;
  workbook format: APPLY exercises plus Reflect pages, teacher-led): Grade 2 Term 1
  only in this band (2025/2026; the French copy carries a 2023/2024 copyright).

---

### W1 — Grade 1, Term 1, new series

- Title as printed: MATHEMATICS, Primary 1, Term 1.
- Publisher: Ministry of Education and Technical Education (Egypt). The printed
  book also carries SPRIX, GPS and CACD (Central Administration for Curriculum
  Development) marks. Editorial team as printed in English: Project Lead and
  Editorial Director Maki Komizo; Editors and Writers Miho Iga, Takayuki Kobayashi,
  Tomoaki Tsunoyama; translator Miki Sakata; Designers Sayaka Higuchi, Shigeyuki
  Hatakeyama. Central Administration for Curriculum Development names on the Term 1
  English file: Mrs. Manal Abbas Ahmed Azkol, Dr. Mohammed Mohey El-Din Abdel Salam,
  Mr. Samir Mohammed Sedawy, Mrs. Eman Sayed Ramadan Mohammed. General Supervision
  Dr. Akram Hassan Mohammed.
- Languages: Arabic, English, French (one work in three languages; two English
  copies — see Editions below).
- Edition/year as printed: copyright "All Rights Reserved © Ministry of Education
  and Technical Education – 2025/2026" in the publisher PDF. The printed-book scan
  is a 2026–2027 printing of the same book (imprint page: print date 2026–2027,
  ISBN 978-633-8475-39-0, deposit number 2026/9978, 100 pages including covers,
  19 × 27 cm).
- Files and pagination:
  - Arabic, publisher PDF (96 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page throughout
    (contents printed p5; content printed pp6–93; Notes printed pp94–95; back cover
    p96). Text layer present.
  - English, publisher PDF (96 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page throughout.
    Text layer present but with spaced-letter font encoding (readable after a
    de-spacing pass).
  - French, publisher PDF (96 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page throughout.
    Text layer present.
  - English, printed-book scan (98 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page + 1 for
    content pages (designed contents on PDF p6; lesson 1-1 on PDF p7 = printed p6;
    Notes on PDF pp95–96 = printed pp94–95; imprint page PDF p97; back cover
    PDF p98). Front cover PDF p1 (cat/dog characters); back cover PDF p98
    (ministry/GPS/SPRIX/CACD logos, class/name box) — the two ends are different
    pages, not a repeated spread. [Checker correction: the draft's "cover spread
    repeated at both ends" was wrong.]
- How it was read: English publisher PDF in full from its text layer, checked
  against the French and Arabic text layers page for page (lesson numbers and PDF
  pages match exactly across all three languages); the English scan checked visually
  at the contents page, lessons 1-1, 6-1 and 8-1, and the imprint page — identical
  chapter list, identical printed pagination, identical lesson artwork and wording.
- Structure: 9 chapters, 40 lessons. Each lesson is normally a teaching page
  (Point! rule box, worked example, How to Solve) plus practice pages (Try,
  Exercise). Chapter openers are the lesson's first page.

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages)

- Chapter 1, Numbers up to 10 (pp6–31). 1-1 How Many? (6–7): colouring the same
  number of pictured things. 1-2 Numbers from 1 to 5 (8–9). 1-3 Let's Count! (from
  1 to 5) (10–11). 1-4 What Makes 5? (12–13): number bonds by colouring some of 5
  circles and counting the rest. 1-5 Numbers from 6 to 10 (14–15). 1-6 Let's Count!
  (from 6 to 10) (16–17). 1-7 What Makes 6? (18–19). 1-8 What Makes 7? (20–21).
  1-9 What Makes 8? (22–23). 1-10 What Makes 9? (24–25). 1-11 What Makes 10?
  (26–27). 1-12 Let's Compare Numbers (28–29): numbers grow to the right on a
  1–10 strip; circle the bigger number. 1-13 The Number Zero (30–31).
- Chapter 2, What's the Position? (pp32–35). 2-1 What's the Position? (32–33):
  first, second, and so on in a line. 2-2 Where Is It? (34–35): describing where a
  thing is (above, below, beside, in front, behind, inside).
- Chapter 3, Addition (pp36–43). 3-1 Addition (Part 1) (36–37): the addition
  sentence form, e.g. "2 + 3 = 5", spoken as "Two plus three is (equals) five";
  the addition and equal signs are named. 3-2 Addition (Part 2) (38–39).
  3-3 Addition with Zero (40–41). 3-4 Making Addition Stories (42–43).
- Chapter 4, Subtraction (pp44–53). 4-1 Subtraction (Part 1) (44–45). 4-2
  Subtraction (Part 2) (46–47). 4-3 Subtraction with Zero (48–49). 4-4 What Is
  the Difference? (50–51): the difference is found by subtracting the smaller
  number from the bigger number, writing both the sentence and the answer.
  4-5 Making Subtraction Stories (52–53).
- Chapter 5, Comparing Lengths (pp54–59). 5-1 Which One Is Longer? (54–55).
  5-2 How Can We Measure? (56–57): measuring when ends cannot be lined up, using
  objects (pencil, finger, tape) and folding paper to compare length and width.
  5-3 Let's Compare Lengths (58–59). All measurement here is by direct comparison
  and non-standard units; no centimetre ruler work yet.
- Chapter 6, Representing Data (pp60–71). 6-1 Representing Data with Pictures
  (60–63): line items up and sort them; mark each one and colour from the bottom
  up. 6-2 What We Learn from Results (64–67). 6-3 Let's Compare Results (68–71).
  Each of these three lessons runs to 4 pages.
- Chapter 7, Numbers Greater than 10 (pp72–85). 7-1 Numbers up to 20 (72–73).
  7-2 Ten and Some Number (from 1 to 10) (74–75): teen numbers split into 10 and
  some number (10 and 4 make 14; 19 is 10 and 9). 7-3 Number Line (76–79, 4 pages).
  7-4 Adding and Subtracting Numbers (Part 1) (80–81). 7-5 Adding and Subtracting
  Numbers (Part 2) (82–83). 7-6 Numbers Greater than 20 (84–85).
- Chapter 8, Reading the Clock (pp86–87). 8-1 Telling Time on a Clock (86–87):
  short hand = hour, long hand = minute; short on 8, long on 12 = 8 o'clock; half
  past is introduced (long hand on 6). O'clock and half past only.
- Chapter 9, Operations with 3 Numbers (pp88–93). 9-1 Adding Three Numbers
  (88–89): work left to right. 9-2 Subtracting Three Numbers (90–91). 9-3
  Calculating with Three Numbers (92–93): mixed three-number calculations.
- Back matter: Notes (ruled) pp94–95; back cover p96 (publisher PDF) carrying
  title, grade, term and year plus print specifications in Arabic.

#### Honest negatives — what W1 does NOT teach

No shape names or shape properties (shapes arrive in Term 2); no money; no mass or
capacity; no multiplication, division or fractions; no 2-digit addition or
subtraction with regrouping (all computation stays within 20, plus teen-number
structure); no standard length units (no cm/mm ruler work); no minutes on the
clock (o'clock and half past only); no pictographs or bar graphs with scales
(picture representation only, unscaled).

#### Held-copy defects and slips

- French file: teaching-page chapter headers on printed pp24–31 read "Les nombers
  jusqu'à 10" (sic, for "nombres"); the contents page spells it correctly.
- English publisher PDF: spaced-letter font encoding in the text layer (a file
  encoding quirk, not a print defect).
- English scan: no duplicated page — PDF p1 is the front cover, PDF p98 the back
  cover (checker correction; the draft's "duplicated cover spread" was wrong).
- No missing content pages in any copy; all 40 lessons present in all languages.

#### Editions (W1) — the Grade 1 Term 1 English trap, resolved

Same work, two printings, not two editions. The publisher PDF (2025/2026
copyright) and the printed-book scan (2026–2027 imprint, ISBN 978-633-8475-39-0)
carry the same 9 chapters, the same printed pagination, and identical lesson
content at every spot-checked opener (1-1, 6-1, 8-1). The front matter differs
only because one is the publisher's PDF (plain contents list, editorial page,
introduction) and the other is the printed book (dedication title page, blank
inside cover, designed contents page, imprint page). The record's "2026" vs
"2026_2027" labels are the two printing years, not two different books.

### W2 — Grade 1, Term 2, new series

- Title as printed: MATHEMATICS, Primary 1, Term 2.
- Publisher: Ministry of Education and Technical Education (Egypt); SPRIX/CACD
  marks as in W1. Editorial team as printed in English: same Japanese team as W1
  (Maki Komizo lead; Miho Iga, Takayuki Kobayashi, Tomoaki Tsunoyama; translator
  Miki Sakata; designers Sayaka Higuchi, Shigeyuki Hatakeyama). Central
  Administration for Curriculum Development names on the Term 2 English file:
  Mrs. Manal Abbas Ahmed Azkol, Dr. Mohammed Mohey El-Din Abdel Salam, Mrs. Sabah
  Abdel Wahed Mohammed, Mr. Sameh Abdel Basset. General Supervision Dr. Akram
  Hassan Mohammed.
- Languages: Arabic, English, French (one work in three languages).
- Edition/year as printed: back cover "MATHEMATICS / Primary 1 / Term 2 /
  2025-2026"; copyright 2025/2026. The Arabic scan's cover likewise reads
  2025–2026; no imprint page carrying a different printing year was found in it.
- Files and pagination:
  - English, publisher PDF (72 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page throughout
    (contents printed p5; content printed pp6–69; Notes pp70–71; back cover p72).
    Text layer present.
  - French, publisher PDF (72 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page throughout;
    contents page matches the English one chapter for chapter with the same
    printed pages. Text layer present.
  - Arabic, printed-book scan (72 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page throughout
    (designed contents on PDF p5; lesson 10-1 on PDF p6 = printed p6; Notes at the
    end). No text layer (image-only scan).
- How it was read: English publisher PDF in full from its text layer (per-page
  lesson headers); French contents page and lesson structure verified from its
  text layer as identical; Arabic scan checked visually at the contents page and
  lesson 10-1 — identical chapter list, identical printed pages, identical lesson
  artwork and wording (ball, cylinder, cuboid, cube shapes in 10-1).
- Structure: 8 chapters (numbered 10–17, continuing Term 1's 1–9), 29 lessons.
  Same Point! / Warm Up / How to Solve / Try / Exercise lesson shape as W1, with
  some lessons running to 3–4 practice pages.

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages)

- Chapter 10, Different Shapes (pp6–9). 10-1 Different Shapes (Part 1) (6–7):
  solid shapes by appearance — ball, cylinder, cuboid, cube; some shapes look the
  same even when turned. 10-2 Different Shapes (Part 2) (8–9): tracing shapes to
  find new shapes.
- Chapter 11, How to Compare (pp10–17). 11-1 Which Has More? (Part 1) (10–11):
  comparing amounts of water by height in identical containers; pour into
  identical containers when the containers differ. 11-2 Which Has More? (Part 2)
  (12–13): comparing by "how many units". 11-3 Which Is Larger? (Part 1) (14–15):
  comparing areas by lining up edges and stacking. 11-4 Which Is Larger? (Part 2)
  (16–17): comparing areas by "how many units".
- Chapter 12, Addition by Making 10 (pp18–21). 12-1 Addition (Part 1) (18–19):
  making-10 for teen sums (9 + 3 = 12 by 9 needing 1, splitting 3 into 1 and 2).
  12-2 Addition (Part 2) (20–21): two ways to make 10 (e.g. 4 + 9).
- Chapter 13, Subtraction by Using 10 (pp22–25). 13-1 Subtraction (Part 1) (22–23):
  15 − 9 = 6 by splitting 15 into 10 and 5 and subtracting from 10. 13-2 Subtraction
  (Part 2) (24–25): two ways (e.g. 12 − 4).
- Chapter 14, Ones, Tens, and Hundreds (pp26–43). 14-1 Making Groups of 10 (26–27).
  14-2 How Many 10s and How Many 1s (28–29): ones place = group of 1s, tens place =
  group of 10s, tens written left, ones right (63 = six 10s, three 1s). 14-3 Ten 10s
  (30–31): ten 10s = one hundred, written 100; 100 is 1 more than 99. 14-4 How
  Numbers are Arranged up to 100 (32–33): the 0–100 chart. 14-5 Number Line up to
  100 (34–35): comparing via the number line (4 more than 70, 2 less than 80).
  14-6 Numbers Bigger than 100 (36–37). 14-7 Adding and Subtracting Ones (Part 1)
  (38–39: e.g. 20 + 5, 38 − 8). 14-8 Adding and Subtracting Ones (Part 2) (40–41):
  ones place first (e.g. 24 + 3). 14-9 Adding and Subtracting Tens (42–43): think
  in tens (e.g. 40 + 10, 100 − 90).
- Chapter 15, Making Shapes (pp44–49). 15-1 Making Shapes (Part 1) (44–45):
  arranging coloured-paper pieces. 15-2 Making Shapes (Part 2) (46–47): arranging
  sticks. 15-3 Making Shapes (Part 3) (48–49): connecting dots with lines.
- Chapter 16, What Time Is It? (pp50–55). 16-1 What Time Is It? (Part 1) (50–53,
  practice spanning 3 pages): hour-hand review, reading o'clock clocks. 16-2 What
  Time Is It? (Part 2) (54–55): long hand = minutes, one mark = 1 minute; when the
  short hand sits between two numbers, read the smaller (e.g. 8:30).
- Chapter 17, Addition and Subtraction (pp56–69). Five lessons of word problems
  with diagrams: 17-1 (Part 1) (56–59): line-position problems ("3rd in line, 7
  behind" gives 3 + 7 = 10; total and position gives the behind-count by
  subtraction, e.g. 13 − 4 = 9). 17-2 (Part 2) (60–63): different-group problems
  recast to one group (6 children + 7 juice boxes; 11 children, 7 chairs — who
  cannot sit). 17-3 (Part 3) (64–65): drawing diagrams with marks to see the
  sentence (7 children, 5 more adults — how many adults). 17-4 (Part 4) (66–67):
  fewer-than problems (12 melons, 4 fewer watermelons). 17-5 (Part 5) (68–69):
  front/behind in a line (5 in front of Yara, 3 behind her).
- Back matter: Notes (ruled) pp70–71; back cover p72 with print specifications in
  Arabic (19 × 27 cm, 4 colours, 70 g text, 180 g cover).

#### Honest negatives — what W2 does NOT teach

No multiplication, division or fractions; no money; no mass or capacity; no
3-digit addition or subtraction with regrouping (computation stays within 100,
plus the idea of numbers bigger than 100); no standard length units beyond the
unit-comparison work of Chapter 11 (no cm/mm ruler work); no shape names or
attributes as a topic (shapes are handled by appearance and composition only);
no bar graphs or scales; no calendar or date work; no perimeter or area formulas
(area is compared, never measured in units squared).

#### Held-copy defects and slips

- English file text slips: "indentical" for "identical" (pp10–11); "substract" in
  a practice header (p22).
- No missing content pages in any copy; all 29 lessons present in all languages.

### W3 — Grade 2, Term 1, OLD series (Education 2.0 "Discover")

- Title as printed: PRIMARY 2, MATHEMATICS, TERM 1.
- Publisher: Ministry of Education and Technical Education (Egypt). The Foreword
  presents the book as part of the "Discover" series produced with Discovery
  Education and the Central Administration for Curriculum Development (CACD),
  under the Education 2.0 reform begun September 2018. This is a different series
  from W4, not a different edition of it.
- Languages: Arabic, English, French (one work in three languages).
- Edition/year as printed: covers read 2025–2026 in all three languages. Copyright:
  English and Arabic copies 2025/2026; the French copy's review page reads
  "Droits d'auteur 2023/2024" — an earlier-copyright printing of the same work.
  No ISBN was found in any copy of this work.
- Files and pagination:
  - English (96 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page throughout (cover p1, blank
    p2, Foreword p3, Contents p4, content printed pp5–91, review/supervision page
    p93, technical-specs page p95, back cover p96; pp92 and p94 blank). Image-only
    scan; read page by page from renders.
  - French (96 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page throughout (Contents on PDF
    p4, same lesson list and printed pages as English except the two corrected
    Chapter 3 pages — see below). Image-only except PDF pp93–96 (review page,
    technical-specs page in Arabic, printer page "مطابع الفتح").
  - Arabic (92 PDF pages): front matter differs (Foreword PDF p3, review/
    supervision page PDF p4, Contents PDF p5); the Contents lists the same 60
    lessons, but its printed pagination runs about 3–4 pages behind the
    English/French (Chapter 1 from printed p2, Chapter 6 ending printed p86, vs
    English Chapter 1 from p5 and Chapter 6 ending p90). The 4-page PDF difference
    (92 vs 96) matches the specs tables: the Arabic specs state 96 pages including
    covers, the English specs 100 pages including covers — each 4 above its PDF
    count, a counting convention, not missing pages. The Arabic lessons were
    verified at Contents level, not page by page.
- How it was read: English file in full, page by page, from rendered page images
  (every lesson header, exercise type and Reflect page seen). French Contents page
  and review page read visually; Arabic Contents page read visually.
- Structure and character: this is a WORKBOOK, not a teaching text. Lessons carry
  no explanations: each opens with APPLY (exercises to do with the teacher or a
  partner) and many close with a Reflect page (write or draw about what was
  learned). Teaching is assumed to happen orally/by the teacher. 6 chapters,
  60 lessons. Money appears as "LE" (Egyptian pounds) in estimation problems.

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages, English/French pagination)

- Chapter 1, data (pp5–18). L1 Reading Data (5): read a birthdays bar graph
  (months Jan–June). L2 Collecting and Representing Data (6–7): build a class bar
  graph of favourite days, plus Reflect. L3 Comparing Data (8): compare day-counts
  with greater-than/less-than/equal circles. L4 Representing and Interpreting
  Data (9–10): build a siblings bar graph, order the counts least to greatest,
  plus Reflect. L5 Representing Data with a scale of 1 (11): favourite-fruit bar
  graph, how-many-more and total questions. L6 Representing Data with a scale of
  2 (12–13): favourite-fruit graph with an axis in 2s, plus Reflect (scale of 1 vs
  scale of 2). L7 Representing Data with a scale of 10 (14): favourite colours of
  200 students, axis in 10s, add/subtract questions. L8 Bar Graph (15): roll two
  dice, shade the sum on a frequency chart ("Which SUM is Rolled the Most?").
  L9 Pictograph (16–17): "Pick a Flower" pictograph with a key (half-flower = 1,
  whole flower = 2), plus Reflect. L10 Graph Elements (18): turn the pictograph
  data into a bar graph, ticking off title, scale, horizontal label, vertical
  label, categories labelled, colourful bars.
- Chapter 2, mental addition and subtraction (pp19–29). L1 Adding Doubles (19):
  doubles 1 + 1 to 10 + 10 with pictures, then near-doubles. L2 Adding or
  Subtracting by Counting (20): counting-on table plus Reflect. L3 Adding or
  Subtracting the number 10 (21): number-chart work plus a Challenge (write your
  own +10 problem). L4 Adding or Subtracting by Making Tens (22–23): make-10
  tables and worked decompositions (6 + 8 = 6 + 4 + 4; 15 − 7 via 15 − 5 = 10),
  plus Reflect. L5 Story Problems on Adding (24): four add-to stories, plus
  Reflect. L6 Story Problems on Subtracting (25): four take-away stories, plus
  Reflect. L7 Mental Applications on Adding (26): missing-addend star problem
  (3 stars at 8 p.m., 13 at 9 p.m.), plus Reflect. L8 Mental Applications on
  Subtracting (27): before/after lunch candy pictures (20 before, 11 after).
  L9 Mental Applications on Adding and Subtracting (28): missing-addend and
  missing-subtrahend sentences, plus Reflect. L10 Adding Using the 120 Chart
  (29): the game "101 and Over" on a 1–120 chart.
- Chapter 3, 3-digit numbers (pp30–40). L1 3-digits Numbers (30): hundreds/tens/
  ones place-value table, teacher-given numbers, worked example 385 (3 hundreds,
  8 tens, 5 ones). L2 More of 3-Digits Numbers (31): place-value game with a
  group, plus Reflect ("How can 3 have different values?"). L3 Standard Form and
  Expanded Form (32): table work (542 = 500 + 40 + 2), plus Reflect with digit
  cards 2, 3, 6. L4 Numbers in Word Form (33): digits 1–9 to words, plus Reflect.
  L5 More Numbers in Word Form (34): teens to words (ten … nineteen). L6 Writing
  Numbers in Different Forms (35): "I have … Who has …?" card game between
  standard and expanded forms. L7 Comparing Numbers (36): compare teacher-given
  numbers with greater-than/less-than/equal circles, plus fill-in comparison
  sentences (576 < ___, 907 < ___, 100 > ___). L8 More of Comparing Numbers
  (37–38): card-pair comparisons, plus Reflect (write directions for comparing).
  L9 Ordering Numbers (39): order sets least-to-greatest and greatest-to-least,
  including 3-digit numbers (156, 241). L10 More of Ordering Numbers (40):
  partner card game, plus Reflect (what I did well / what I am still working on).
- Chapter 4, adding and subtracting with regrouping (pp41–58). L1 Commutative
  Property in Addition (41): solve, then rewrite by switching addends. L2 More of
  Mental Applications on Adding and Subtracting (42–43): dice-and-card game
  (two dice digits + one card digit, add or subtract), plus Reflect. L3 Decomposing
  Numbers Into Ones and Tens (44–45): tens/ones tables and number-bond trees
  (42, 37, 56, 22, 62, 85). L4 Adding Without Regrouping (46–48): decomposition
  method with tens sticks and ones dots plus number bonds (23 + 35 = 58 worked),
  story problems, plus Reflect. L5 Subtracting without Regrouping (49–51):
  decomposition method with crossing out (37 − 25 = 12 worked), story problems,
  plus Reflect (write your own story problems). L6 Estimating The Sum and The
  difference (52): estimate by place value, explicitly "Do not solve the problems"
  (including LE money and minutes stories). L7 Comparing The Sum and The
  Estimation (53): estimate from tens digits, decompose, find the sum, judge
  whether estimate and sum are close. L8 Adding By Regrouping Ones (54–55):
  draw tens sticks and ones dots, circle 10 ones to regroup (26 + 38 worked),
  plus Reflect ("too many Tens in the Tens place"). L9 More of Adding By
  Regrouping Ones (56): card-generated addends, regroup if needed. L10 Adding
  more than Two Numbers By Regrouping Ones (57–58): pair-and-add method
  (25 + 14 + 35 + 17 = 91 worked by pairing).
- Chapter 5, geometry and length (pp59–72). L1 Attributes of 2-dimensional Shapes
  (59): sides/vertices table for triangle, square, rectangle, trapezoid, rhombus,
  pentagon, hexagon; star the quadrilaterals. (The Contents lists this lesson as
  "Attributed of 2-dimensional Shapes" — sic.) L2 Sorting 2-dimensional Shapes
  (60): colour/circle/cross-out by attribute rules, plus Reflect. L3 Drawing
  Geometric Shapes (61–62): draw shapes the teacher describes, plus Reflect (can
  a shape have two sides? two vertices? ten sides?). L4 Creating a Picture Using
  2-dimensional Shapes (63): compose a picture (sun, cat, flower, train) from
  given shapes. L5 Measuring The Length In Centimeters (64–65): ruler readings
  for crayon, paper clip, eraser, pencil, glue stick (vertical rulers on p65).
  L6 Estimating The Length (66): name objects about 1 cm, 10 cm, 50 cm, 100 cm,
  plus Reflect. L7 Measuring The Side Length of a Geometric Shape (67): measure
  one side of each shape (triangle, square, rhombus, rectangle short/long sides,
  trapezoid short/long sides, pentagon, hexagon) and record in cm. L8 Attributes
  of 3-Dimensional Shapes (68): faces/edges/vertices table for square-based
  pyramid, cylinder, sphere, cube, rectangular prism, plus Reflect. L9 Sorting
  3-dimensional Shapes (69–71): copy shapes into an attribute chart (4 or more
  faces; 0 edges/faces/vertices; 1 vertex; 6 or more edges; at least 1 circle
  face; more than 2 faces but fewer than 6), plus Reflect. L10 Creating
  3-dimensional Shapes (72): make 3D shapes, name each and write one property.
- Chapter 6, mass and time (pp73–91). L1 Measuring Mass (73–74): choose grams or
  kilograms for scissors, motorcycle, goat, shoes, feather, fox, balloons, chair,
  plus Reflect. L2 Units of Measuring Mass (75): name things measurable by 1 gram,
  half a kilogram, 1 kg, 5 kg, 10 kg, 100 kg. L3 Applications on Measuring Mass
  (76–78): addition word problems in gm/kg (pets 10 kg + 5 kg; two 100-gram balls;
  bicycle 12 kg + tricycle 9 kg; sand buckets 65 g + 26 g), plus Reflect. L4 More
  Applications on Measuring Mass (79–81): rocks 19 kg + 7 kg; sugar 80 g − 20 g;
  seashell bags 4 + 5 kg and 6 + 5 kg combined; crackers 78 g − 19 g; then write
  your own mass word problems (p81). L5 Time a.m. Or p.m. (82–83): circle a.m. or
  p.m. for pictured activities with clocks, plus Reflect. L6 Creating An Analog
  Clock (84): clock-face model — split into hours, draw hour and minute hands,
  read and write the time. (Contents prints "Creating An Analog Cock" — sic.)
  L7 Reading Time With Halves (85): in this copy the page is a Reflect page only
  ("Write or draw something you learned about telling time to the half hour") —
  no APPLY exercise page for half-hour reading appears; whether the lesson is
  meant to be teacher-led or an exercise page is missing from this copy could not
  be settled from the file alone. [Checker: the English sequence leaves no gap
  for a missed page — L6 APPLY p84, L7 Reflect p85, L8 APPLY p86 — and the
  French copy's p85 is the same Reflect-only page ("Leçon 7 … Réfléchis").] L8 Applications on Time (86): teacher-dictated
  times shown on analog and digital clocks. L9 Reading Time in Minutes (87–89):
  count triangles drawn in one minute, plus a minute-mark clock diagram, plus
  Reflect on the minute hand (p89). L10 More Applications on Time (90–91):
  match clocks to quarter past / quarter to times (12:45, 3:15, 4:45, 7:15,
  2:15), plus closing Reflect (p91).
- End matter: blank p92; review/supervision page p93 (English revisers: Ms. Manal
  Abass Ahmed Azkol, Dr. Mohamed Mohyeldin Abdesalam Abouraia, Ahmed Ibrahim
  El-Desouky Hashim, George Yuhanna Meikheil Gerges, Mr. Samir Mohamed Sedawy
  Mohamed, Sabah Abdelwahed Mohamed; Instructional Supervision Dr. Akram Hassan
  Mohamed; Copyright © 2025/2026); blank p94; technical-specs page p95 in Arabic;
  back cover p96 (CACD, Egypt Vision 2030, Afifi Group logos).

#### Honest negatives — what W3 does NOT teach

No written vertical algorithms at all (all addition/subtraction is mental,
decomposition or sticks-and-dots regrouping — the vertical method arrives only in
the new series, W4); no multiplication, division or fractions; no capacity
(litres/decilitres/millilitres); no gram–kilogram conversion (units are chosen,
never converted: 1 kg = 1000 g is not taught); no elapsed time or duration
(clock reading and a.m./p.m. only); no millimetres or metres (length is
centimetres only); no data work beyond Chapter 1's graphs; no 3-digit addition
or subtraction with regrouping (regrouping stays 2-digit).

#### Held-copy defects and slips

- English Contents page errors, both proved against the actual lesson pages:
  Chapter 3 Lesson 6 is listed as p36 but sits on p35; Chapter 3 Lesson 7
  Comparing Numbers is listed as "??" and sits on p36. (The French Contents page
  gets both right: L6 p35, L7 p36.)
- English Contents: Chapter 6 Lesson 6 "Creating An Analog Cock" (sic, for
  "Clock"); Chapter 5 Lesson 1 "Attributed of 2-dimensional Shapes" (sic, for
  "Attributes").
- Chapter 6 Lesson 9 minute-mark clock diagram: the 11 o'clock position is
  labelled ":50" a second time instead of ":55".
- Chapter 6 Lesson 7 presents as a Reflect-only page (see map above); flagged,
  not asserted as a missing page.
- French Contents header reads "CONTENU" (sic, missing the final T).
- Specs-vs-PDF counts: English specs state 100 pages including covers against 96
  PDF pages; Arabic specs state 96 against 92 PDF pages — a uniform +4 counting
  convention (covers/flyleaves), not missing content.
- No other missing pages: all 60 lessons are present in the English copy.

### W4 — Grade 2, Term 1, NEW series

- Title as printed: MATHEMATICS, Primary 2, Term 1.
- Publisher: Ministry of Education and Technical Education (Egypt); SPRIX/CACD
  marks. Editorial team as printed in English (imprint page, PDF p2): Project
  Lead and Editorial Director Maki Komizo; Editors and Writers Miho Iga, Takayuki
  Kobayashi, Tomoaki Tsunoyama; Designers Sayaka Higuchi, Shigeyuki Hatakeyama.
  [Checker correction: no translator is credited on the W4 imprint page — the
  name Miki Sakata appears on the W1/W2 imprint pages, not here.]
  Central Administration for Curriculum Development names:
  Mrs. Manal Abbas Ahmed Azkol, Dr. Mohammed Mohey El-Din Abdel Salam, Mrs. Eman
  Sayed Ramadan Mohammed, Mr. Samir Mohammed Sedawy, Mr. Sameh Abdel Basset,
  Mrs. Sabah Abdel Wahed Mohammed. General Supervision Dr. Gebriel Anwer Hemieda
  (different from the Grade 1 books' Dr. Akram Hassan Mohammed). Copyright line:
  "All Rights Reserved ©Ministry of Education and Technical Education -
  2026/2027".
- Languages: Arabic, English (one work in two languages; no French new-series
  file in this band).
- Edition/year as printed: 2026/2027 in both languages (English imprint page;
  Arabic back-cover specs table: print date 2026–2027, deposit number 2026/14470).
  ISBN 978-633-8530-14-3 in BOTH languages (English back-cover specs table;
  Arabic back-cover specs table) — same edition, same ISBN. Specs: 132 pages
  including covers, 19 × 27 cm, 4 colours, 80 g text in both; cover 200 g in the
  English copy, 180 g in the Arabic copy. [Checker correction: the draft's
  "180 g cover in both" was wrong for the English copy.] Printers
  differ by language: English copy, October Engineering Press; Arabic copy,
  Al-Hadaya Press – Al-Arabi Anwar Sayed.
- Files and pagination:
  - English, printed-book scan (128 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page + 4 for
    content pages (cover PDF p1; imprint PDF p2; characters PDF p5; blank PDF p6;
    Contents PDF p7; lesson 1-1 on PDF p8 = printed p4; last lesson page PDF p125
    = printed p121; Notes PDF pp126–127; back cover PDF p128). Verified at both
    ends (printed p4 = PDF p8; printed p121 = PDF p125). No text layer.
  - Arabic, printed-book scan (128 PDF pages): PDF page = printed page + 2 for
    content pages (Contents PDF p7; lesson 1-1 on PDF p8 = printed p6). The
    Arabic chapter pages run exactly 2 above the English throughout (Ch1 printed
    p6 vs English p4; Ch9 printed p120 vs English p118). No text layer.
- How it was read: English file in full, page by page, from rendered page images
  (every lesson header, Point! box, Warm Up, Try and Exercise seen). Arabic
  Contents page and lesson 1-1 read visually (same vehicle-count graph exercise,
  same structure) and its back-cover specs table read visually.
- Structure: 9 chapters, 41 lessons. Same lesson shape as W1/W2 (Point! / Warm Up
  / How to Solve / Try / Exercise), but lesson lengths vary: some lessons take 4
  printed pages, some compress How to Solve + Try + Exercise onto a second page.
  Money appears as EGP in word problems. A fixed side tab strip numbered 1–17 runs
  down teaching pages with the current chapter's number highlighted (the strip
  matches the Grade 1 year's chapter numbering 1–9 plus 10–17; this reading of the
  strip is an inference, flagged as such).

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages, English pagination; Arabic = +2 throughout)

- Chapter 1, Graphs and Charts (pp4–15). 1-1 Graphs and Charts (4–7): Grade 1
  organising reviewed (mark each item, colour boxes from the bottom); a graph
  (circles stacked from the bottom, lined up) vs a chart (numbers shown
  directly); vehicle-count graph exercise. 1-2 Let's Read Graphs and Charts
  (8–11): survey data — counting, comparing, most/least, how-many-more questions;
  an excursion destination survey exercise. [Checker correction: the lesson's
  own title is "Let's Read Graphs and Charts", not "Graphs and Charts".]
  1-3 Let's Compare Graphs (12–15): comparing two graphs — Warm Up compares
  Class 1 vs Class 2 fruit surveys, Try compares two vehicle-preference surveys,
  Exercise compares Class 1 vs Class 2 pet surveys. [Checker correction: the
  lesson's own title is "Let's Compare Graphs", and all four of its pages were
  read — no favourite-colours survey appears in it; the draft's "survey about
  favourite colours" was not found and is replaced by the surveys above.]
- Chapter 2, Vertical Addition (pp16–29). 2-1 Vertical Addition (Part 1) (16–19):
  adding 2-digit numbers without regrouping in vertical form, ones place first,
  with place-value boxes. 2-2 Vertical Addition (Part 2) (20–21): adding a 1-digit
  number to a 2-digit number vertically (e.g. 3 + 24). 2-3 Vertical Addition
  (Part 3) (22–23): adding with regrouping in the ones place (opener example
  26 + 17, carrying 1 to the tens place). [Checker correction: the whole lesson
  (printed pp22–23) was read and contains no 24 + 8.] 2-4 Vertical Addition (Part 4) (24–25): adding two 2-digit
  numbers with regrouping (e.g. 36 + 27). 2-5 Vertical Addition (Part 5) (26–29):
  adding with regrouping where the tens sum also regroups (e.g. 48 + 37), plus
  story problems.
- Chapter 3, Vertical Subtraction (pp30–47). 3-1 Vertical Subtraction (Part 1)
  (30–33): subtracting without borrowing, ones place first. 3-2 Vertical
  Subtraction (Part 2) (34–37): subtracting a 1-digit number from a 2-digit
  number without borrowing. 3-3 Vertical Subtraction (Part 3) (38–41): borrowing
  from the tens place (opener example 46 − 28). [Checker correction: the whole
  lesson (printed pp38–41) was read and contains no 52 − 28 as a problem; 52
  appears only as a subtrahend in 80 − 52.] 3-4 Vertical Subtraction
  (Part 4) (42–43):
  2-digit minus 1-digit with borrowing (54 − 6); when the tens-place calculation
  gives 0, nothing is written in the tens place (54 − 46 = 8, not 08). 3-5 The
  relation between the subtraction and the addition (44–47): check a subtraction
  by adding difference + subtrahend to get the minuend (54 − 26 = 28 because
  28 + 26 = 54), with total/remaining/got-off bar diagrams.
- Chapter 4, Time and Duration (pp48–55). 4-1 Time and Duration (Part 1) (48–51):
  duration defined as the interval between one time and another; one minute = one
  long-hand scale mark; one full long-hand rotation = 1 hour = 60 minutes;
  durations from clock diagrams (waking up 8:00, leaving home 8:45, arriving at
  the park 9:00); times before/after; hour–minute conversions (1 hour 20 minutes
  = 80 minutes; 90 minutes = 1 hour 30 minutes). 4-2 Time and Duration (Part 2)
  (52–55): before 12 o'clock noon = AM, after = PM, 12 o'clock noon sometimes
  called Noon; AM and PM each last 12 hours; 1 day = 24 hours; writing times with
  AM/PM from a day timeline and from clocks.
- Chapter 5, Units of Length (pp56–67). 5-1 Units of Length (Part 1) (56–57):
  length = how many 1 centimetres; centimetre written cm; counting from a
  non-zero mark (3 parts of 1 cm from a mark = 3 cm). 5-2 Units of Length
  (Part 2) (58–59): 1 cm split into 10 equal lengths = 1 millimetre, written mm;
  1 cm = 10 mm; readings like 4 cm 7 mm and 9 cm 3 mm. 5-3 Units of Length
  (Part 3) (60–61): a straight line vs not a line; converting with 1 cm = 10 mm
  (8 cm = 80 mm; 3 cm 2 mm = 32 mm; 45 mm = 4 cm 5 mm). 5-4 How to Draw Straight
  Line (62–63): five steps with a ruler (starting point, align 0 mark, ending
  point, align straight edge, draw). 5-5 Calculating Lengths (64–67): calculate
  like units together (cm with cm, mm with mm); bent-line lengths by adding
  segments; which line is longer, and by how much.
- Chapter 6, Numbers Bigger than 100 (pp68–97). 6-1 Numbers Bigger Than 100
  (Part 1) (68–71): hundreds place (234 = 2 hundreds, 3 tens, 4 ones), reading and
  writing 3-digit numbers from sticks/paper models. 6-2 Numbers Bigger Than 100
  (Part 2) (72–75): zero in the tens place (204 = two hundreds, 0 tens, 4 ones;
  also 500, 800). 6-3 Numbers Bigger Than 100 (Part 3) (76–79): place value —
  a digit 0–9 fits one place, 10 does not; 10 tens combine into 1 hundred;
  expanded form (402 = 400 + 2). 6-4 Numbers Bigger Than 100 (Part 4) (80–83):
  thinking in tens (13 tens = 130; 240 = 24 tens; how many tens make 280, 520,
  740; how many ones make 180, 238). 6-5 Numbers Bigger Than 100 (Part 5)
  (84–87): reading number lines to 1000 — first check what one small scale
  represents (1 or 10). 6-6 Numbers Bigger Than 100 (Part 6) (88–89): ten 100s =
  One Thousand, written 1,000; one number expressed different ways (640 = 600 +
  40; 640 = 700 − 60; 640 = 64 tens). 6-7 Numbers Bigger Than 100 (Part 7)
  (90–91): multiples of ten and of one hundred by groups of 10/100 (60 + 50 =
  110; 500 − 200 = 300). 6-8 Numbers Bigger Than 100 (Part 8) (92–93): hundreds/
  tens/ones calculations by place value (300 + 20 = 320; 430 − 30 = 400). 6-9
  Numbers Bigger Than 100 (Part 9) (94–95): comparing by largest place value
  first, with greater-than/less-than symbols (359 > 298; 359 < 362). 6-10 Numbers
  Bigger Than 100 (Part 10) (96–97): comparing a number with an expression
  (=, >, <; 120 > 50 + 60; 120 = 50 + 70); can-Sara-buy-it money problems in EGP.
- Chapter 7, Units of Capacity (pp98–103). 7-1 Deciliter (dL) and Capacity
  (98–99): capacity = how many times the 1-decilitre unit fits; decilitre
  abbreviated dL; a 1 dL measuring cup; a bottle holding 5 dL. 7-2 Liter (L) and
  Capacity (100–101): litre for large capacity, abbreviated L; 1 L = 10 dL;
  reading 1 L measuring cups (1 L 4 dL = 14 dL); conversions (7 L = 70 dL;
  40 dL = 4 L). 7-3 Milliliter (mL) and Capacity (102–103): millilitre for
  capacity smaller than dL, abbreviated mL; 1 L = 1,000 mL; 1 dL = 100 mL; a
  1 dL cup divided into 10 ticks, one tick = 10 mL (3 dL 50 mL = 350 mL);
  capacity calculations must use the same unit (1 L 5 dL + 3 L = 4 L 5 dL).
- Chapter 8, Vertical Addition and Subtraction (pp104–117). 8-1 Vertical Addition
  and Subtraction (Part 1) (104–105): carrying from tens to hundreds (74 + 63 =
  137). 8-2 Vertical Addition and Subtraction (Part 2) (106–107): carrying in
  both ones and tens places (74 + 68 = 142). 8-3 Vertical Addition and
  Subtraction (Part 3) (108–109): borrowing from hundreds to tens (137 − 54 =
  83). 8-4 Vertical Addition and Subtraction (Part 4) (110–111): borrowing in
  both ones and tens places (137 − 59 = 78). 8-5 Vertical Addition and
  Subtraction (Part 5) (112–113): borrowing across a zero tens place — first take
  1 from hundreds to tens, then 1 from tens to ones (107 − 59 = 48). 8-6 Vertical
  Addition and Subtraction (Part 6) (114–117): mixed 3-digit/2-digit vertical
  work, aligning place values and starting from the ones place (658 + 24 = 682;
  541 − 27 = 514).
- Chapter 9, Mental Math Strategies (pp118–121). 9-1 Mental Math Strategies
  (Part 1) (118–119): in addition the answer stays the same when the order
  changes; parentheses show the group to calculate first (12 + (7 + 3) = 22;
  reorder to make 10s or multiples of 10). 9-2 Mental Math Strategies (Part 2)
  (120–121): splitting numbers before calculating (37 + 9 by splitting the
  summand or the addend to make a multiple of ten; 73 − 8 by splitting the
  minuend or the subtrahend); "calculate mentally without using vertical
  calculation".
- Back matter: Notes (ruled) pp122–123; back cover PDF p128 with the specs table
  (English copy: October Engineering Press; Arabic copy: Al-Hadaya Press).

#### Honest negatives — what W4 does NOT teach

No multiplication, division or fractions; no mass/weight (grams/kilograms never
appear); no shape work at all (no 2D or 3D shape lessons — geometry is limited to
measuring/drawing straight lines); no money as a topic (EGP appears only inside
word problems, with no coins, notes or change); no pictographs (graphs and
charts only, in Chapter 1); no calendar/date work; no perimeter or area formulas;
no temperature; no data handling beyond Chapter 1.

#### Held-copy defects and slips

- Lesson 1-2 Exercise: the chart header reads "Vegetable" above an Excursion
  Destination Survey (a copied header from another exercise).
- Lesson 3-5's Try instruction (printed p46) spells "substracion" (sic, missing
  the second t): "Match the addition mathematical sentence and the substracion
  that checks the result". [Checker correction: the draft's "matching headers in
  lessons 3-5 and 7-3" was wrong on both counts — it is a Try instruction, not a
  header, and lesson 7-3 was read in full (printed pp102–103) and carries no
  such spelling.]
- No missing content pages; all 41 lessons present in both languages. The two
  scans' pagination offsets (English +4, Arabic +2) are front-matter counting
  differences, not missing pages.

### Record corrections the covers prove

1. **Grade 2 Term 1 is NOT one book appearing twice — it is two different series.**
   The record's "2026 vs 2026_2027" pairs for Grade 2 Term 1 (Arabic twice,
   English twice) do not describe duplicate editions. The 2026 files are the old
   Education 2.0 "Discover" workbook (W3, blue school-objects cover, 60 workbook
   lessons, no vertical algorithms). The 2026_2027 files are the new SPRIX/CACD
   series (W4, cat/dog characters, 41 taught lessons, vertical algorithms,
   capacity, duration). Any count that merges them understates Grade 2 Term 1 by
   a whole series.
2. **Grade 1 Term 1 English is one work in two printings, not two editions.** The
   2026 file (publisher PDF, 2025/2026 copyright) and the 2026_2027 file
   (printed-book scan, 2026–2027 imprint, ISBN 978-633-8475-39-0) are the same
   book with the same chapters, pagination and lesson content; the year labels
   are printing years. Counting them as two books would double-count W1.
3. **Record titles, terms and languages for all 12 files check out against the
   covers** — no file is misfiled by grade, term or language. The only
   year-precision notes: W3's French copy carries a 2023/2024 copyright against
   the English/Arabic 2025/2026 (same work, earlier printing), and W1's English
   scan is a 2026–2027 printing against the publisher PDF's 2025/2026.
4. **Old-series English Contents page errors (W3):** Chapter 3 Lesson 6 is listed
   as p36 but sits on p35; Chapter 3 Lesson 7's page is printed as "??" and sits
   on p36. The French Contents page has both right (p35, p36). Any index built
   from the English Contents page alone will misplace those two lessons.
5. **Pagination offsets are per-file facts, not errors:** W1 English scan
   PDF = printed + 1 (publisher PDFs PDF = printed); W2 Arabic scan PDF = printed
   (publisher PDFs PDF = printed); W3 Arabic printed pagination runs ~3–4 pages
   behind English/French with 92 vs 96 PDF pages; W4 English scan PDF = printed
   + 4, Arabic scan PDF = printed + 2, with Arabic chapter pages exactly 2 above
   English. W3 specs tables state 4 more pages than their PDFs (100 vs 96;
   96 vs 92) — a covers/flyleaves counting convention, not missing pages.
6. **W4 has one ISBN across languages** (978-633-8530-14-3, English and Arabic,
   2026/2027); **W1's printed Term 1 scan has ISBN 978-633-8475-39-0** (the
   publisher PDFs carry no ISBN in their text layers); **W3 has no ISBN found**
   in any copy.

### What could not be opened or verified

Nothing in this band failed to open: all 12 PDFs passed the magic check and were
read. The limits of verification, stated plainly:

- W3 Arabic and French were verified at Contents level plus covers (French
  Contents matches English lesson for lesson; Arabic Contents lists the same 60
  lessons with its own pagination) — they were not read page by page, the
  English copy was.
- W4 Arabic was verified at Contents level, lesson 1-1, and back-cover specs —
  not page by page, the English copy was.
- W1 Arabic and French, and W2 French, were verified by exact text-layer
  page-for-page match against the English files, plus visual spot-checks for W1
  English scan and W2 Arabic scan — not by independent full visual reads.
- W3 Chapter 6 Lesson 7's Reflect-only presentation could not be settled as
  "by design" vs "exercise page missing from this copy" from the file alone.
- The W4 side tab strip's meaning (1–17 with the current chapter highlighted) is
  recorded as an inference, not a book-stated fact.
- The W1 English scan's imprint book code digits were not transcribed with
  certainty and are therefore not quoted here; its ISBN, deposit number and
  print date are quoted as read.

## Band B — Grades 3–4

Band reader draft. No commit made from this file; it is a draft for the parent
orchestrator only. All maths content below is filed in English, in my own
summaries. Short quoted fragments are lesson headings or a single worked line
only; nothing is transcribed at length.

### Method, in brief

- Each PDF was checked first for `%PDF` magic and a `pdfinfo` page count
  (table below). Kind, grade, term and language were proved from the cover
  and the title/imprint page of each file, not from the filename.
- Text-layer books (Grade 3 old English Term 1 and Term 2, Grade 3 new
  English Term 1, Grade 4 French Term 1) were scanned page by page through
  their text layers, with lesson openers read in full.
- Image-only books were read visually: cover, contents and sampled lesson
  pages at the start, middle and end of each book, plus folio checks.
  Parallel language editions were then clustered into one work (see
  "Clustering") and the work's content filed once; the spot-checks for each
  parallel edition are listed under its work.
- Page numbers: "printed" means the folio printed on the page; "PDF" means
  the page number inside the PDF file. The two are NOT the same in most of
  these books, and the rule is stated per work and verified at both ends
  of each book. Contents-list numbers are lesson-start numbers, and in
  three books they disagree with the body folios — flagged where it
  happens, never silently "corrected". [CHECKED: in Work 1 the English
  contents disagree with the body headers at two lessons (see the
  Work 1 map notes); the Arabic contents agree with the body at both.]

All ten files open, start with `%PDF`, and render. MD5s are given so a
later copy can be checked against the exact file read here.

### Trap resolutions (the set questions, answered from the books)

1. **Grade 3 Term 1 Arabic twice, English twice — same edition twice? NO.
   Two different editions.** The "2026" files are the green-cover
   Discovery-series book for 2025–2026 (Work 1 below). The "2026_2027"
   files are a completely different orange-cover SPRIX book for
   2026/2027 (Work 2 below): different chapter order, different lessons,
   different teaching method, different characters and layout. Merging
   them would lose a whole book. The Arabic and English copies inside
   each edition do match each other (see spot-checks).
2. **Grade 3 Term 2 in French? NO — not in this set, and not hiding
   anywhere in it.** Term 2 exists here in Arabic and English only
   (Work 3). The French Term 1 file is Term 1 only: its cover says
   1er semestre and its contents end at capacity (Chapter 6). Neither
   Term 1 file contains Term 2 material, and no file in the band is a
   mislabelled French Term 2.
3. **Grade 4 Term 2 hiding in any file? NO.** All three Grade 4 files
   are Term 1 / 1er semestre only, 136 PDF pages each, and all three end
   with Unit 8 (order of operations) followed by revision credits /
   back-cover print matter. No second-term unit starts anywhere in them.
4. **A further gap the books themselves reveal:** the new SPRIX Grade 3
   Term 1 book labels its last chapter "Vertical Multiplication
   (Part 1)". Its Part 2 — and a new-series Term 2 generally — is not
   in this band. The old series, by contrast, is complete across the two
   terms (Term 1 Chapters 1–6, Term 2 Chapters 7–12, one continuous
   numbering).

### Work 1 — Grade 3, Term 1, old (Discovery) edition, 2025–2026

- Title as printed: English "PRIMARY 3 MATHEMATICS, TERM 1"; Arabic
  "Primary 3, Mathematics, First term"; French "3ème PRIMAIRE,
  MATHÉMATIQUES, 1er SEMESTRE".
- Publisher: Ministry of Education and Technical Education (Egypt),
  Central Administration for Curriculum Development (CACD). The
  English copyright page says Copyright 2024/2025, all rights reserved
  to the Ministry; revisers include Manal Abass Ahmed Azkol
  (Mathematical Consultant) and Mohamed Mohyeldin Abdesalam Abouraia.
  The series foreword presents this as part of the Education 2.0 /
  "Discover" transformation.
- Languages: Arabic, English, French — three files, one work.
- Edition/year as printed: 2025–2026 (all three covers).
- PDF pages: Arabic 113, English 112, French 112. The Arabic file's
  extra page is a back-cover leaf carrying CACD / Egypt Vision logos;
  its body is the same as the others.
- Pagination, verified: English and Arabic, PDF = printed + 4 for the
  body (PDF 6 = printed 2; PDF 111 = printed 107; PDF 112 = copyright
  credits). French, PDF = printed + 4 as well, but its body starts at
  printed 1 (PDF 5 = printed 1; PDF 52 = printed 48), so French folios
  run 1 below the English/Arabic folios for the same lesson. The
  contents lists give lesson-start printed pages; English and Arabic
  contents agree exactly, French contents are the English numbers
  minus 1 throughout.
- Teaching shape: every lesson runs CONNECT, then APPLY, then REFLECT,
  with CHALLENGE items. Pupils work with a shoulder partner, counters,
  digit cards, a 120 chart, fraction-style strips and arrays; most
  lessons are activity-first (build it, draw it, explain it) rather
  than rule-first. Money appears in Egyptian pounds (LE).
- Blank leaves (not missing content): English PDF 2 and PDF 4 are
  blank; Arabic PDF 4 is blank.

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages; English numbering, French −1)

- Chapter 1 (printed 2–10): patterns; bar graphs; line plots; measuring
  length in centimetres, metres, millimetres. Lessons: Patterns 2;
  More of Bar Graphs 3 in the contents, body header folio 4 (PDF 8;
  pupils build a bar graph from sibling data) [CHECKED: contents-vs-body
  mismatch — the lesson's header page is folio 4, and the Arabic
  contents give 4];
  Line Plot 5; Measuring Lengths in Centimeter 6; in Meter 8; in
  Millimeter 10 (heading spelled "LENGHTHS" in the book — see Slips).
- Chapter 2 (printed 11–25): thousands; ten thousands and hundred
  thousands; numbers in different forms (standard, expanded, word
  forms); arrays; multiplication as repeated addition and arrays;
  commutative property. Lessons: Thousands 11; More of Thousands 13;
  Ten Thousands – Hundred Thousands 15; Numbers in Different Forms 17;
  Arrays 18; Multiplication 21; Commutative Property 23.
- Chapter 3 (printed 26–43): multiplication word problems and
  applications; multiples of 2, 3, 5, 10 (120 chart work); factors
  through arrays; telling time (analogue and digital, including
  "who has the later time" games); applications on time; division as
  sharing and grouping (part-part-whole models); applications on
  division; the multiplication–division relation and fact families.
  Lessons: Word Problems on Multiplication 26; Applications on
  Multiplication 28; Multiples of 2 & 3, 29; Multiples of 5 & 10, 31;
  Factors of a Number Using Arrays 33; Time 34; Applications on Time
  35; Division 37; Applications on Division 40; Relation Between
  Multiplication and Division 42.
- Chapter 4 (printed 44–61): polygons (naming, sorting by vertices);
  properties of quadrilaterals (including trapezium, defined by the
  pupil in their own words, and a cut-out quadrilaterals page);
  area by counting squares and by arrays; rectangles with equal area;
  area using models; area by splitting arrays; distributive property
  of multiplication (via arrays). Lessons: Polygons 44; Properties of
  Quadrilaterals 46; Area 49; Rectangles With Equal Area 51; Area
  Using Models 54; Area By Splitting Arrays 58; Distributive Property
  on Multiplication 59.
- Chapter 5 (printed 62–82): perimeter of polygons; perimeter and area
  together; area from dimensions; area by different strategies;
  different perimeters for the same area; different areas for the same
  perimeter; applications on perimeter and area; multiplying by
  multiples of 10. Lessons: Perimeter of Polygons 62; Perimeter and
  Area 64; Area Using The Dimensions 67; Area Using Different
  Strategies 69; Different Perimeters for The Same Area 72; Different
  Areas for The Same Perimeter 75; Applications on Perimeter and
  Area 77; Multiplying by Multiples of 10, 80.
- Chapter 6 (printed 83–107): patterns for multiplying by multiples of
  10; strategies for multiplying by 9 (list of equations, 120 chart,
  tens facts); multiplication-and-addition facts; comparing and
  ordering numbers in different forms; addition strategies;
  subtraction strategies (checked by an addition problem);
  applications on addition and subtraction (multi-digit story
  problems in LE); capacity in millilitres and litres (choosing the
  unit, graduated cylinders, naming containers and their volumes).
  Lessons: Patterns of Multiplying by Multiples of 10, 83; Strategies
  of Multiplying by 9, 86; Facts on Multiplication and Addition 90;
  Comparing and Ordering Numbers in Different Forms 93; Addition
  Strategies 95; Subtraction Strategies 97; Applications on Addition
  and Subtraction 98 in the contents, body header folio 99 (PDF 103;
  the Mahmoud chickens problem, 5,350 eggs) [CHECKED: contents-vs-body
  mismatch — the Arabic contents give 99]; Capacity 102; Reading
  Capacity 104. The body
  runs to printed 107 (reflection pages after the last lesson start);
  PDF 112 is the copyright/credits page.
- Honest negatives — this volume does NOT teach: fractions (they are
  Term 2); decimals; division with remainders as a named topic;
  formal column addition/subtraction (strategies only — the vertical
  method arrives in the new SPRIX book, Work 2, and in Grade 4);
  angles, lines or rays; mass or temperature; 3-D volume; division
  beyond sharing/grouping with small facts; any multiplication fact
  beyond the tables used for arrays and multiples of 10.
- Parallel-edition spot-checks: Arabic contents (PDF 5) matches the
  English contents lesson-for-lesson except at the two lessons above,
  where the Arabic contents agree with the body headers (Bar Graphs 4,
  Applications on Addition and Subtraction 99) and the English
  contents are one low [CHECKED]; Arabic Chapter 1 Lesson 1 (PDF 6,
  printed 2) is the same leaf-pattern and number-pattern page as the
  English (30, 40, 50, 60, 70; 52, 54, 56, 58). French contents
  (PDF 4) matches with the −1 folio shift (its Bar Graphs entry, like
  the English contents, reads 3 against a body header of folio 4);
  French Chapter 4 Lesson 3 on area (PDF 52, printed 48) is the same
  rectangle-counting lesson as the English. No content difference was
  found between the three languages.

### Work 2 — Grade 3, Term 1, new (SPRIX) edition, 2026/2027

- Title as printed: English "PRIMARY 3, Term 1, MATHEMATICS"; Arabic
  "Primary 3, Mathematics, First term". The English file's PDF
  metadata title is an Arabic production leftover mentioning grade 2
  ("الرياضيات ص2 ب ف1"); the Arabic file's metadata title is blank.
  The cover, the contents and every lesson are Grade 3 — the metadata
  is wrong, the book is not. [CHECKED: pdfinfo on both files; the
  draft's first version wrongly attributed the grade-2 metadata to
  the Arabic file.]
- Publisher: Ministry of Education and Technical Education, with
  SPRIX (cover logo "EDUCATION SPRINGS NEW LIFE") and the Central
  Administration for Curriculum Development. Editorial team (PDF 2):
  project lead Maki Komizo; editors/writers Miho Iga, Takayuki
  Kobayashi, Tomoaki Tsunoyama; designers Sayaka Higuchi, Shigeyuki
  Hatakeyama; Ministry side Manal Abbas Ahmed Azkol, Mohammed Mohey
  El-Din Abdel Salam, Eman Sayed Ramadan Mohammed; general supervision
  Gebriel Anwer Hemieda. Imprint: "All Rights Reserved © Ministry of
  Education and Technical Education – 2026/2027". The Arabic copy's
  back page gives print date 2026–2027, 120 pages, size 19×27 cm, and
  ISBN 978-633-8530-20-4; the English copy carries no ISBN in its
  file (it ends with two blank Notes pages and a blank final page).
- Languages: Arabic, English — two files, one work. No French copy of
  this edition is in the band.
- PDF pages: 120 each. Pagination, verified: PDF = printed for the
  body (PDF 8 = printed 8; PDF 54 = printed 54; PDF 61 = printed 61;
  PDF 115 = printed 115). Front matter is PDF 1–7 (cover, editorial
  page, blank leaves, characters page, contents, blank leaf). WARNING:
  the contents list's chapter-start numbers run about 4–5 below the
  body folios (contents says Chapter 1 at 4, Chapter 2 at 16, Chapter 10
  at 102; the teaching pages sit at folios 8, 20 and 106). The ranges
  below use body folios, which were checked on the page. [CHECKED:
  the draft's 21 and 107 were the exercise pages following the
  Chapter 2 and Chapter 10 teach pages; the Arabic contents give the
  body starts 8, 20, 32, 44, 54, 62, 76, 82, 92, 106.]
- Teaching shape, quite different from Work 1: each lesson is a
  two-page spread in the order Point! (the rule, boxed), Warm Up,
  How to Solve (worked examples with the reasoning written out), then
  Try and Exercise pages, and each chapter ends with a "Power Up"
  Review and Practice spread. Three mascot characters (Brainy, Smarty,
  Genius in English; the same three animals in Arabic) comment in
  speech bubbles. The side margin of every page carries a 1–18 number
  strip (a times-table aid). Money is in EGP. Several teaching pages
  are page images with no text layer; exercise/review pages mostly
  carry text.
- Blank leaves: English PDF 3, 4 and 7 are blank; PDF 118–119 are
  headed Notes (blank); PDF 120 is blank.

#### Chapter-by-chapter map (body folios = PDF pages)

- Chapter 1, Rules of Multiplication (folios 8–19): 1-1 Properties of
  Multiplication Part 1 (changing the multiplier by 1 changes the
  answer by the multiplicand; order can be switched); 1-2 Properties
  Part 2; 1-3 Multiplying by 10 and 0 Part 1; 1-4 Part 2; 1-5 Find the
  Missing Numbers; Power Up review 18–19 [CHECKED: folio 20 is
  Chapter 2's teach page, not Chapter 1 review].
- Chapter 2, Time and Duration (folios 20–31): 2-1 Finding Time Before
  and After Part 1; 2-2 Part 2; 2-3 Shorter Time Units (converting
  between hours, minutes and seconds in both directions); review
  30–31. No clock-face telling-time teaching of the Work 1 kind —
  this chapter assumes time can be read and works on before/after
  and unit conversion.
- Chapter 3, Division (folios 32–43): 3-1 Division (sharing equally);
  3-2 Sharing and Division; 3-3 Scenarios for Division (which
  situations are division situations); 3-4 Division to Compare (how
  many times as many / as long, e.g. ribbon lengths). [CHECKED —
  CORRECTED: folio 40 is 3-4's teach page (the 15 cm ribbon is
  5 times the 3 cm ribbon, 15 ÷ 3 = 5), not an unheaded practice
  page; no unheaded practice page exists in this chapter.]
  Division here is exact only; remainders wait until Chapter 8.
- Chapter 4, Addition and Subtraction (folios 44–53): 4-1 Adding 3-
  and 4-Digit Numbers by the vertical method (line up by place value,
  start from ones, carry 1 when a sum reaches 10); 4-2 Subtracting
  3- and 4-Digit Numbers Part 1; 4-3 Part 2 (borrowing, including
  across zeros as practised); 4-4 Addition and Subtraction (mixed
  story problems: book counts, prices in EGP, change from 200 EGP,
  pages read and left). Power Up review 52–53.
- Chapter 5, Length (folios 54–61): 5-1 Longer Units of Length
  (measuring tape, smallest mark 1 cm, 1 km = 1,000 m, converting
  km/m and reading tape scales in m and cm); 5-2 Distance and
  Shortest Distance (distance along a path vs straight-line shortest
  distance, on maps; choosing mm, cm, m or km for a length). The
  exercise-page running header shortens the 5-2 title to "Distance
  and Route". Power Up review 60–61.
- Chapter 6, Organizing Data (folios 62–75): 6-1 Tally marks and
  tables (counting tally groups, favourite subjects/fruits); 6-2
  Reading Bar Graphs (scale marks, most/least, how many more);
  6-3 Drawing Bar Graphs (from a table); 6-4 Organizing Data in
  Tables (two-way tables with row and column totals, e.g. books
  borrowed by class, injuries by cause and month). Power Up review
  74–75.
- Chapter 7, Mental Math (folios 76–81): 7-1 Mental Math for Addition;
  7-2 Mental Math for Subtraction; review 80–81. (Short chapter:
  mental strategies only, no new written method.)
- Chapter 8, Division with Remainders (folios 82–91): 8-1 Division
  with a Remainder (17 ÷ 3 = 5 remainder 2; use the divisor's table,
  take the largest product below the dividend; remainder = dividend
  minus that product; the remainder must always be smaller than the
  divisor); 8-2 Checking the Answer (remainder smaller than divisor,
  then divisor × quotient + remainder = dividend); 8-3 Using
  Division with a Remainder Part 1 (attaching the right unit to
  quotient and remainder — people vs pieces left over); 8-4 Part 2
  (deciding from the question whether to round up, e.g. plates or
  seats needed, or to ignore the remainder, e.g. full boxes only).
  Power Up review 90–91.
- Chapter 9, Numbers Bigger than 10,000 (folios 92–105): 9-1 How to
  Write Big Numbers; 9-2 The Structure of Big Numbers (place value
  beyond ten thousands); 9-3 Comparing Big Numbers; 9-4 Numbers
  Multiplied by 10 and Divided by 10; Power Up review 104–105
  [CHECKED — CORRECTED: folios 104–105 are Chapter 9's Power Up
  spread in the same Try/Exercise pattern as the other chapters, not
  a separate non-Power-Up practice].
- Chapter 10, Vertical Multiplication, Part 1 (folios 106–117): 10-1
  Multiplying by Tens and Hundreds; 10-2 Vertical Multiplication,
  2-digit × 1-digit; 10-3 Vertical Multiplication, 3-digit ×
  1-digit; 10-4 Rules of Multiplication; 10-5 Using Multiplication
  (story problems); Power Up review 116–117. The "(Part 1)" label
  means multi-digit × multi-digit multiplication is not in this
  volume.
- Honest negatives — this volume does NOT teach: fractions; decimals;
  geometry (no polygons, area or perimeter); mass, capacity or
  temperature; bar-graph work beyond Chapter 6; telling the time
  from a clock face; division by a 2-digit divisor; multiplication
  by a 2-digit number; any Term 2 content.
- Parallel-edition spot-checks: Arabic contents (PDF 7) lists the
  same ten chapters; its chapter numbers are the English contents
  numbers + 4 (Chapter 1 at 8, Chapter 10 at 106), matching the body
  folios. Arabic Chapter 5 Lesson 1 (PDF 54, folio 54) is the same
  measuring-tape / kilometre lesson as the English, and Arabic
  Chapter 1 Lesson 1 exercises (PDF 9, folio 9) are the same
  missing-number items as the English. No content difference found.

- Title as printed: English "PRIMARY 3, MATHEMATICS, TERM 2"; Arabic

- PDF pages: 128 each. Pagination, verified: PDF = printed exactly
  (PDF 5 = printed 5; PDF 127 = printed 127; PDF 128 = credits/code
  page). The contents page is printed 4; the foreword is PDF 3 and

- Teaching shape: the same CONNECT / APPLY / REFLECT / CHALLENGE

- Contents order note: the contents page's text layer is
  column-scrambled (Chapter 10's heading and lessons are interleaved
  with Chapter 7's, and Chapter 9's with Chapter 7's), but the
  printed page groups lessons by chapter and the book itself runs in
