  before Chapter 7 as a whole in the text layer.]

#### Chapter-by-chapter map (printed pages = PDF pages)

- Chapter 7 (printed 5–24): associative property of multiplication;
  distributive property; estimating multiplication (round, then
  solve); applications on multiplication and division; strategies
  for multiplication and division; perimeter of a square and a
  rectangle; two-step story problems; strategies for solving two-step
  problems; writing story problems. Lessons: Associative Property 5;
  Distributive Property 7; Estimating Multiplication 9; Applications
  on Multiplication and Division 12; Strategies for Multiplication
  and Division 15; Perimeter of a Square and a Rectangle 17; Two-Step
  Story Problems 19; Strategies for Solving Two-Step Story Problems
  21; Writing Story Problems 23.
- Chapter 8 (printed 25–44): fractions from the start — what equal
  parts mean; unit fractions (exploring, modelling, comparing with
  models); which is bigger; expressing one whole with unit fractions;
  the relation between fractions and division (sharing counters
  gives a fraction of the whole); more of that relation; applications
  on fractions. Lessons: More Fractions 25; Exploring Unit Fractions
  28; Applications on Unit Fractions Using Models 30; Comparing Unit
  Fractions Using Models 32; Which Is Bigger? 35; Expressing One
  Using the Unit Fractions 37; Relation Between Fractions and
  Division 39; More of the Relation 41; Applications on Fractions 43.
- Chapter 9 (printed 45–66): fractions on a number line; comparing
  unit fractions on a number line; comparing fractions with models;
  comparing fractions on a number line; comparing two fractions with
  the same numerator or denominator (pupils form and test a
  hypothesis); adding two fractions with the same denominator;
  subtracting two fractions with the same denominator; story
  problems on adding and subtracting fractions. Lessons:
  Representing Fractions on a Number Line 45; Comparing Unit
  Fractions Using a Number Line 47; Comparing Fractions Using
  Models 51; Comparing Fractions Using a Number Line 53; Comparing
  Two Fractions with the Same Numerator or Denominator 55; Adding
  Two Fractions with the Same Denominator 58; Subtracting Two
  Fractions with the Same Denominator 61; Story Problems on Adding
  and Subtracting Fractions 63.
- Chapter 10 (printed 67–91): equivalent fractions of a half; more
  equivalent fractions; patterns of equivalent fractions; equivalent
  fractions on the number line; applications on equivalent fractions;
  dividing using bar models; story problems on division; the
  relation between multiplication and division (fact families).
  Lessons: Equivalent Fractions of a Half 67; More of Equivalent
  Fractions 72; Patterns of Equivalent Fractions 76; Equivalent
  Fractions with the Number Line 79; Applications on Equivalent
  Fractions 81; Dividing Using the Bar Models 83; Story Problems on
  Division 86; Relation Between Multiplication and Division 89.
- Chapter 11 (printed 92–111): multiplication facts with different
  strategies (fluent facts first); story problems on multiplication
  and division (including finding an unknown number of tickets at
  8 LE each from a total of 72 LE); creating story problems on
  multiplication; creating story problems on division; story
  problems on perimeter and area; the perimeter for a given area and
  side length; applications on perimeter and area (including a
  "my dream house" floor-plan task). Lessons: Multiplication Facts with
  Different Strategies 92; Story Problems on Multiplication and
  Division 94; Creating Story Problems on Multiplication 98;
  Creating Story Problems on Division 100; Story Problems on the
  Perimeter and the Area 102; Perimeter for a Given Area and a Side
  Length 106; Applications on the Perimeter and the Area 110.
  [CHECKED — CORRECTED: the Chapter 11 entry previously also listed
  "shading exactly one half of squares in different ways"; that task
  is Chapter 12 Lesson 1 only (PDF 112) and is removed here.]
- Chapter 12 (printed 112–127): creating halves in non-routine ways
  (shading half of a square in different shapes); ordering fractions
  on the number line; applications on numbers (smallest/largest
  numbers from given digits); elapsed time (start/end times, road
  trips, after-school routines, with analogue clocks); applications
  on data representations (collecting data, then building a line
  plot and a bar graph with titles and labelled axes). Lessons:
  Creating Halves with Non-Routine Ways 112; Ordering Fractions
  Using the Number Line 115; Applications on Numbers 118; Elapsed
  Time 121; Applications on Data Representations 125. The body runs
  to printed 127 (the data project continues past the last lesson
  start); PDF 128 is credits/code.
- Honest negatives — this volume does NOT teach: decimals; fractions
  with different denominators (addition/subtraction only with the
  same denominator); improper fractions or mixed numbers as a topic;
  division with remainders as a named topic (division stays exact,
  modelled with bars); multi-digit multiplication or division;
  geometry beyond perimeter and area; measurement of length, mass
  or capacity (those were Term 1); angles; negative numbers.
- Parallel-edition spot-checks: Arabic contents (PDF 4) lists the
  same Chapters 7–12 with the same lesson-start numbers as the
  English contents; Arabic Chapter 7 Lesson 1 (PDF 5, printed 5) is
  the same associative-property rectangle task as the English.
  No content difference found.

- Title as printed: English "Math Term 1" (cover; "Primary 4 Student

- Publisher: Discovery Education (Techbook) for the Ministry of

  record years differ between the French copy (2025–2026) and the
  English/Arabic copies (2026–2027) although the content is one book.
- Languages: Arabic, English, French — three files, one work.
- PDF pages: 136 each. Pagination, verified: English and Arabic,

- Teaching shape: a Discovery Techbook. Each lesson has Learning

  a small illustration of a phone showing an English lesson page;
  the English words in its text layer come from that illustration,
  not from a misbound English page.

#### Unit-by-unit map (body folios, English/Arabic numbering)

- Theme 1, Number Sense and Operations.
- Unit 1, Place Value (folios 3–21 in English/Arabic; 1–19 in French):
  Concept 1.1 Reinforcing Place Value — Big Numbers (to the
  milliards/billions place, periods of ones, thousands, millions,
  milliards); Changing Place Values; Many Forms to Write Numbers;
  Composing and Decomposing. Concept 1.2 Using Place Value —
  Comparing Big Numbers; Comparing Numbers in Multiple Forms;
  Descending and Ascending Numbers; Rounding Rules (rounding to a
  given place). Lessons 1–8, starting at folios 3, 5, 7, 9, 12, 14,
  17, 20 (contents numbers; body folios match these in English and
  Arabic).
- Unit 2, Addition and Subtraction Strategies (folios 24–37):
  Concept 2.1 — Properties of Addition; Addition with Regrouping;
  Subtraction with Regrouping. Concept 2.2 Solving Multistep
  Problems — Bar Models, Variables and Story Problems; Solving
  Multistep Story Problems with Addition and Subtraction. Lessons
  starting at folios 24, 27, 29, 32, 35.
- Unit 3, Measurement Concepts (folios 40–57): Concept 3.1 Metric
  Measurement — Measuring Length; Measuring Mass; Capacity units.
  Concept 3.2 Measurement of Time — Time Units; Elapsed Time;
  Application of Measurement 1 and 2. Lessons starting at folios 40,
  43, 45, 48, 50, 53, 56 (contents numbers; the two "Application"
  lessons are mixed measurement story problems).
- Unit 4, Area and Perimeter (folios 59–67): Finding the Perimeter;
  Finding the Area; Unknown Dimensions (find a missing side from
  area or perimeter); Complex Shapes (split a composite shape,
  find area and perimeter of each part, add). Lessons starting at
  folios 59, 62, 64, 66 (contents numbers).
- Theme 2, Mathematical Operations and Algebraic Thinking.
- Unit 5, Multiplication as a Relation (folios 71–83 in
  English/Arabic; 69–81 in French): Concept 5.1
  Multiplicative Comparisons — Comparison with Multiplication
  ("times as many"); Creating a Multiplicative Comparison Equation;
  Solving a Multiplicative Comparison Equation. Concept 5.2
  Properties and Models — Commutative Property; Identity and Zero
  Properties; Associative Property; Applying Models in
  Multiplication. Lessons starting at folios 71, 73, 75, 77, 79,
  81, 83 in English/Arabic (69, 71, 73, 75, 77, 79, 81 in French)
  [CHECKED — CORRECTED: the draft listed the French numbers as
  English/Arabic folios].
- Unit 6, Factors and Multiples (folios 86–97 in English/Arabic;
  84–95 in French): Concept 6.1
  Understanding Factors — Identifying Factors of Whole Numbers;
  Prime and Composite Numbers; Greatest Common Factor (PGCD in
  French). Concept 6.2 Understanding Multiples — Identifying
  Multiples; Common Multiples; Relations Between Factors and
  Multiples. Lessons starting at folios 86, 88, 91, 93, 95, 97 in
  English/Arabic (84, 86, 89, 91, 93, 95 in French) [CHECKED —
  CORRECTED: same French-numbers error as Unit 5].
- Unit 7, Multiplication and Division: Calculation and Relations
  (folios 100–122 in English/Arabic; 98–120 in French): Concept 7.1
  Multiplication by 1- and 2-Digit
  Factors — Area Model Strategy; Distributive Property; Partial
  Products Algorithm; Multiplication by One Digit (standard
  algorithm, 1-digit × up to 4-digit, with estimation first);
  Multiplication of a 2-Digit Number by a Multiple of 10 (the
  contents/header shortens this title to "…de 1" — see Slips).
  Concept 7.2 Division by One Digit — Exploring Remainders;
  Division Models; Area Model and Division; Partial Quotients
  Algorithm; Standard Division Algorithm; Division and
  Multiplication (the inverse relation, checking by multiplication).
  Lessons starting at folios 100, 102, 104, 107, 109, 111, 113, 116,
  117, 119, 121 in English/Arabic (98, 100, 102, 105, 107, 109, 111,
  114, 115, 117, 119 in French) [CHECKED — CORRECTED: same
  French-numbers error; English/Arabic Unit 7 closes at folio 122
  (PDF 130, the 1,429 ÷ 7 division close), French at folio 120].
- Unit 8, Order of Operations (folios 124–127 in English/Arabic;
  122–125 in French): Order of Operations [CHECKED — CORRECTED:
  the draft's 122–125 / 120–125 mixed contents numbers with body
  folios; Lesson 1 teaches at folio 124 (English/Arabic) / 122
  (French), Lesson 2 at 126 / 124, closing at 127 / 125].
  (brackets first, then multiplication/division, then

- Honest negatives — this Term 1 volume does NOT teach: fractions
  (no fraction unit, although the parent letter names fractions as

  (no 2-digit × 2-digit); division by a 2-digit divisor; multi-step
  work beyond addition/subtraction bar models and the order of
  operations; any Term 2 unit.

  English Unit 1 Lesson 1 (PDF 11, printed 3) and French Unit 1

### Record corrections (proved from covers / imprint pages)

No record title, grade, term or language in this band is wrong — all
ten check out against their covers. The corrections are narrower:

1. **Year labels should be school years, not single years.** The
   record's "2026" for the old-series books and the French Grade 4
   book is shorthand: every one of those covers says 2025–2026
   (French Grade 4 colophon: print run 2025/2026). Recommend
   recording 2025–2026 for: G3 old T1 (all languages), G3 old T2
   (both languages), G4 French. The record's "2026_2027" for the new
   SPRIX G3 books and for G4 Arabic/English is confirmed by their
   imprints and back covers (SPRIX imprint 2026/2027; G4 English
   and Arabic back covers dated 2026–2027) — no change there.
2. **The two G3 Term 1 pairs are not duplicates.** If any downstream
   count merged the "2026" and "2026_2027" G3 Term 1 files as the
   same book, it undercounts by one whole work (two files). They are
   different editions (Discovery green 2025–2026 vs SPRIX orange
   2026/2027).
3. **No French G3 Term 2 and no G4 Term 2 should be inferred.** The
   band holds no such file, and no held file contains such content
   under another label. Any grid row expecting them stays open on
   this evidence.
4. **Grade 4 is one work, not three, across its record years.** The
   French copy's 2025–2026 dating and the English/Arabic copies'
   2026–2027 dating describe reprint years of the same ©2025
   Discovery Techbook (same units, same lessons, same ISBN family
   per language). They must not be counted as separate editions of
   the content.
5. **Metadata, not record, but worth fixing at source:** the new
   SPRIX English G3 file's PDF metadata title mentions grade 2.
   The book itself is Grade 3 throughout; only the metadata string
   is wrong.

## Band C — Grades 5–6

Band reader's draft. No repo commits were made; this file is the only deliverable.
All four works are Discovery Education "Techbook" material written for the
Egyptian Ministry of Education and Technical Education (MOETE) under the
Education 2.0 framework. Maths content below is filed in English even where the
book read is Arabic or French; the book's language is noted per edition.

### How this band was read

- Every PDF was verified first: `%PDF` magic plus `pdfinfo` page count. Kind,
  grade, term and language were proved from each book's COVER and title/imprint
  page, not from filenames or record titles.
- The English edition of each work was read IN FULL, page by page. Grade 5
  English editions are image-only scans and were read from rendered page images
  (contact sheets); Grade 6 English editions have a text layer and were read from
  it, cross-checked against page renders for folios, figures and end matter.
- The Arabic and French parallel editions were NOT read cover to cover in each
  language. They were verified section-for-section against the English by a full
  table-of-contents comparison (every unit, concept, lesson title and printed page
  number) plus sampled lesson pages rendered and read. Where a sampled page is
  cited, it is cited. Anything the spot-check did not cover is said so, not assumed.
- Lesson pattern, Grade 5: Learning Targets, ACCESS (Egypt-context reading),
  BUILD (activities, often Whiteboard tasks), CONNECT ("Math around Egypt" /
  "Math at Work" / Writing About Math), then "Check Your Understanding —
  Follow your teacher's instructions" (teacher-directed; no printed answers).
  Each lesson opener carries a QR code to the digital Techbook.
- Lesson pattern, Grade 6: Learning Target plus "Classroom Practices" (Grade 5
  has "Student Behaviors" in Term 2) or "Student Behaviors" box, ACCESS, then a
  combined BUILD AND CONNECT, "Let's Chat" discussions, and Quick Codes
  (egm6xxx; Grade 5 uses egm5xxx and separate BUILD and CONNECT sections).
- No word-for-word transcription is used below: own summaries and structural
  notes, with only very short quoted fragments (a heading phrase, a worked
  example). Quoted fragments are in quotation marks.
- "Printed p." always means the folio printed on the page. "PDF p." means the
  page number inside the held PDF file. PDF page = printed page is NEVER assumed;
  each offset below was verified from folios visible on body pages.

### Set-verification traps — resolutions

1. **No French Term 2 exists in this band — confirmed.** The 10 files account
   for every slot: Grade 5 Term 2 exists only in Arabic and English, Grade 6
   Term 2 only in Arabic and English. A grep of the work folder's records file
   for Grades 5–6 returns exactly these 10 files, and the only French files among
   them are the two Term 1 files. No French Term 2 is hiding misfiled in any
   file: every file's cover, TOC and sampled interior matches its listed
   grade/term/language.
2. **Grade 6 Term 1 Arabic (2026_2027 file) is the SAME edition as the
   2025-2026 English/French.** Evidence from the book itself: the back-cover
   barcode (PDF p. 122) carries ISBN 978-1-61629-986-6, identical to the
   English/French Term 1 ISBN; the back-matter imprint page (PDF p. 121)
   carries an Arabic print table stating book size 19×27 cm, text paper 70 g,
   cover 180 g, 4 colours, 124 pages, print year 2026-2027, a deposit number
   beginning 2026/10/, a local ISBN 978-633-8475-45-1, and printer
   "دار الرحمن للطباعة" (Dar Al-Rahman). The Arabic TOC (printed pp. iv–v)
   matches the English TOC lesson-for-lesson and page-for-page (see Work 3).
   So the Arabic copy is the 2026-2027 school-year printing of the same 2025
   Discovery course; the English/French copies are 2025-2026 printings.
3. **Grade 6 Term 2 has no French edition in this band — confirmed** the same
   way as trap 1: Arabic and English only, covers and TOCs agree, nothing
   misfiled.

  lesson page identical to English) plus parent-letter pages. French text layer
  is noisy OCR (duplicated lines, misspellings such as "postion" for position,
  cover-art text bleeding into the TOC); it was used only for structure
  cross-checking, never as a text source.
- Foreword content: MOETE Education 2.0 history (KG rollout September 2018,
  Primary 4 in 2021, rollout continuing to 2030); thanks to CCIMD (Center for
  Curriculum and Instructional Materials Development) and partners Discovery
  Education, National Geographic Learning, Nahdet Masr, Longman Egypt,
  UNICEF, UNESCO, World Bank.

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages; 46 lessons)

**Theme 1 | Number Sense and Operations.**

- **Unit 1 — Decimal Place Value and Computation.**
  - Concept 1.1 Decimals to the Thousandths Place: L1 Decimals to the
    Thousandths Place pp. 3–6 (place-value grids; Fayoum birds weights); L2
    Place Value Shuffle pp. 7–10 (multiplying/dividing by 10 shifts digits;
    place-value charts); L3 Composing and Decomposing Decimals pp. 11–14
    (expanded forms; place-value charts); L4 Comparing Decimals pp. 15–16
    (>, <, = by place value); L5 Rounding Decimals pp. 17–19 (number-line
    midpoint and rounding-rule strategies; Wadi El Rayan contexts).
  - Concept 1.2 Adding and Subtracting Decimals: L6 Estimating Decimal Sums
    pp. 20–21 (benchmark numbers, front-end estimation, shadoof context); L7
    Modeling Decimal Addition pp. 22–28 (grid models, place-value charts,
    Whiteboard modelling; Nile context); L8 Modeling Decimal Subtraction
    pp. 29–33 (X-crossed grid models, algorithm connection; Nile perch/catfish
    error analysis); L9 Estimating Decimal Differences pp. 34–36 (Nile Delta
    papyrus data; Abu Simbel connect); L10 Subtracting to the Thousandths
    Place pp. 37–39 (subtracting in thousandths units, reasonableness checks,
    "Spin a Difference" game; Ganges/Mississippi deltas); L11 Decimal Story
    Problems pp. 40–42 (Tahya Misr Bridge, Nile fish table, world bridges table).
- **Unit 2 — Number Relationships.**
  - Concept 2.1 Expressions, Equations, and the Real World: L1 Expressions,
    Equations, and Variables pp. 44–46 (expression vs equation, unknowns as
    letters; Sinai/Gulf of Suez/Aqaba context; sort into Equations / Expressions
    / Neither); L2 Variables in Equations pp. 47–48 (solving one-step decimal
    equations by inverse operations; Ras Muhammad connect); L3 Telling Stories
    with Numbers pp. 49–50 (writing/solving decimal story problems; error
    analysis on adding instead of subtracting).
  - Concept 2.2 Factors and Multiples: L4 Prime Factorization pp. 51–53 (prime
    vs composite, factor trees; Suez Canal connect — 193 prime, 12 not a factor
    of 193, 1 neither); L5 Greatest Common Factors (G.C.F.) pp. 54–55 (GCF via
    factor trees/lists; Red Sea diving context); L6 Identifying Multiples
    pp. 56–58 (multiples, common multiples, Venn diagrams; Ras Abu Galum bus
    schedules); L7 Least Common Multiple (L.C.M.) pp. 59–61 (LCM by listing
    multiples; kofta/aish baladi, bike laps, mangroves); L8 Factors or
    Multiples? pp. 62–63 (choosing GCF vs LCM in word problems; Sinai Trail).
- **Unit 3 — Multiplication with Whole Numbers.**
  - Concept 3.1 Multiplying by 2-Digit Numbers: L1 Using the Area Model to
    Multiply pp. 65–67 (area model, expanded form, e.g. 234×27; Eastern Desert
    buses); L2 The Distributive Property of Multiplication pp. 68–71 (area
    model = distributive property; Badir's 206×45 error — 206 decomposed as
    200+60+0; fennec fox connect); L3 Multiplying by a 2-digit number using
    the algorithm pp. 72–74 (standard algorithm, comparing three strategies;
    45×37=1,665; Eastern Desert rain connect); L4 Multiplying Multi-Digit
    Numbers pp. 75–77 (4-digit × 2-digit algorithm, estimation checks; Ashraf's
    357×36 error analysis); L5 Multiplication Problems in the Real World
    pp. 78–79 (multistep stories: sandstorms, Mona's restaurant
    kebabs/baklava/tahini/lemonade).

**Theme 2 | Mathematical Operations and Algebraic Thinking.**

- **Unit 4 — Division with Whole Numbers.**
  - Concept 4.1 Models for Division: L1 Dividing Using Area Model pp. 81–82
    (division by area model with remainders, e.g. 3,622÷31=116 R26; error
    analysis on 2,852÷24); L2 Estimating Quotients pp. 83–84 (compatible
    numbers; "Winner Takes All" game).
  - Concept 4.2 Dividing by 2-Digit Divisors: L3 Using the division algorithm
    pp. 85–86 (standard algorithm by 2-digit divisor; coffee beans; cookie
    bags); L4 The relation between division and multiplication pp. 87–88
    (checking division by multiplication; "Keep the Leftovers" game; Ziad's
    buttons remainder sense; Ayman's 8,858÷43 error — claimed 26, working
    implies 206); L5 Multistep Story Problems pp. 89–90 (remainder
    interpretation: baklava trays round up; steel prices, paper reams, quilts,
    T-shirts, road trip).
- **Unit 5 — Multiplication and Division with Decimals.**
  - Concept 5.1 Multiplying Decimals: L1 Multiplying by Powers of Ten
    pp. 92–94 (×10/100/1000 and ×0.1/0.01/0.001 patterns; pencil scaling; Hoda's
    stride); L2 Multiplying Decimals by Whole Numbers pp. 95–96 (number-line
    models; "Make the Greatest Product" game; bookstore connect); L3
    Multiplying Tenths by Tenths pp. 97–98 (Base-10 grids; Kamal/Nadia debate
    — tenths × tenths lands in hundredths); L4 Multiplying decimals using the
    area model pp. 99–100 (decimal area models; cinder blocks connect); L5
    Multiplying Decimals through the Hundredths Place pp. 101–102 (standard
    algorithm, decimal-point placement reasoning; 42.16×3.4 discussion); L6
    Multiplying Decimals through the Thousandths Place pp. 103–104 (algorithm
    to thousandths; architects' decimal points); L7 Decimals and the Metric
    System pp. 105–107 (choosing units mm/cm/m/km; conversion tables for
    length, mass, capacity; vet cat 3.648 kg vs 3,648.0 g); L8 Measurement,
    Decimals, and Powers of Ten pp. 108–109 (converting via × powers of ten;
    weightlifter Mohamed Ehab); L9 Solving Multistep Story Problems
    pp. 110–111 (computer repair, sugar-cane juice, growth, bandages,
    circuit-board area).
  - Concept 5.2 Dividing Decimals: L10 Dividing by Powers of Ten pp. 112–113
    (÷10/100/1000 and ÷0.1/0.01/0.001 patterns; glass-blowing temperature
    connect); L11 Patterns and Relationships in Powers of Ten pp. 114–115
    (×/÷ inverse pairs; juice-container connect); L12 Dividing Decimals by
    Whole Numbers pp. 116–117 (standard algorithm to thousandths; remainders
    become decimal quotients; hibiscus); L13 Dividing Decimals by Decimals
    pp. 118–119 (making the divisor whole; error analysis on 77.43÷0.3 where
    only one number was shifted).
- **Unit 6 — Numerical Expressions and Patterns.**
  - Concept 6.1 Evaluating Numerical Expressions and Patterns: L1 Ordering of
    Mathematical Operations pp. 121–122 (×/÷ left-to-right, then +/−
    left-to-right, no parentheses first; bus-route connect); L2 Numerical
    Expressions with Parentheses pp. 123–125 (parentheses, then brackets;
    grouping symbols change value); L3 Writing Expressions to Represent
    Scenarios pp. 126–127 (translating multi-step verbal directions into
    expressions; Kamel's savings, cycling rate, vases); L4 Identifying Numerical
    Patterns pp. 128–131 (tile pattern stages, input/output tables, rules with
    variables; Yahia's n×7 vs Walid's n÷7).

#### Honest negatives (Work 1)

No fractions (those are Term 2); no geometry, coordinate planes, or volume in
Term 1 (those are Term 2); no negative numbers; no exponents beyond
powers-of-ten patterns; no printed answer key (all "Check Your Understanding"
items are teacher-directed).

#### Held-copy defects and slips (Work 1, English scan unless noted)

- CONFIRMED at 200 dpi: Lesson 7 opener (printed p. 22) shows an EMPTY QR box
  outline where every other lesson opener carries a QR code — the QR graphic
  is missing in the held copy, not merely faint.
- CONFIRMED at 200 dpi: Lesson 8 (printed p. 32), Whiteboard item 7 reads
  "4.14  3.09 =" with NO operator between the numbers. The lesson is Modeling
  Decimal Subtraction and the neighbouring item is "0.2 − 0.05", so the
  intended item is "4.14 − 3.09" — the minus sign is dropped in the held copy.
  Checker addition: the defect is English-scan-only. The Arabic parallel
  (printed p. 32, PDF p. 43) reads "4.14 − 3.09 =" with the minus present, and
  the French parallel (printed p. 32, PDF p. 40) reads "4,14 − 3,09 =" with
  the minus present.
- Imprint slip (PDF p. 140): "Ms. Manal Abass Ahmed Azkol (Math cmatical
  Cosultaut)" — typesetting garble for "Mathematical Consultant".
- Minor text slip (printed p. 22, ACCESS passage): "and they meet each other
  in Khartoum" begins with a lowercase "and" after a full stop.
- Print-table count vs held file: the English imprint's Arabic print table
  lists 144 (its header reads "number of pages with the cover") while the held
  PDF has 140 pages. The lesson run is complete (pp. 3–131 plus imprint
  present) and no content gap was found; the 4-page difference is unexplained

- Title as printed: English "Math Term 2" (Primary 5, Student Edition); Arabic
  "الرياضيات / الفصل الدراسي الثاني" (Grade 5 pupil book).

  by Discovery Education under the Education 2.0 framework. The parent letter
  names the "Primary 5 Mathematics Techbook" and its ACCESS / BUILD / CONNECT
  lesson structure, WRAP-UP / PRACTICE / CHECK YOUR UNDERSTANDING features,
  and QR/Quick codes to the digital Techbook.
- Languages/editions: Arabic 2025-2026; English 2025-2026. NO French edition
  exists in this band (see trap 1). English back imprint (PDF p. 136): Revised
  by Dr. Mohamed Mohyeldin Abdelsalam, Dr. Osama Abdelazim Abdelsalam Mohamed,
  Mr. Ahmed Ibrahim El-Dosouyky Hashim, Mr. Samir Mohamed Sedawy, Mrs. Sabah

  Consultant; Supervision Dr. Akram Hassan Mohamed; CACD and Egypt Vision 2030

  the held English copy — recorded as "not visible", not as missing content.
- Pagination: English PDF 136 pages; lessons printed pp. 3–127; PDF page =
  printed page + 8, verified at printed pp. 3 and 127. Front matter: cover
  PDF p. 1; name page PDF p. 2; Foreword printed p. iii (PDF p. 3; MOETE
  Education 2.0 history as in Term 1); blank PDF p. 4 (printed p. iv leaf);
  parent letter printed pp. v–vii (PDF pp. 5–7; states "major work of Primary 5
  includes multiplication and division of whole numbers, fractions, mixed
  numbers, and decimals, as well as numerical expressions", plus patterns,

  136 pages; TOC printed pp. viii–ix on PDF pp. 8–9 (Foreword p. iii, letter

  lesson-for-lesson and page-for-page with English (all 39 lessons, identical
  printed pages), plus Unit 7 Lesson 1 sampled (printed p. 3: finding like
  denominators with the multiplication chart, same pairs as English).
  Numbering continues from Term 1: Term 1 = Units 1–6, Term 2 starts at Unit 7.

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages; 39 lessons)

**Theme 3 | Fractions, Decimals, and Proportional Relationships.**

- **Unit 7 — Adding and Subtracting Fractions.**
  - Concept 7.1 Adding and Subtracting Fractions with Unlike Denominators: L1
    Finding Like Denominators Using the LCM pp. 3–4 (multiplication chart,
    smallest like denominator); L2 Using Models to Add and Subtract Fractions
    with Unlike Denominators pp. 5–6 (fraction wall to twelfths; garden error
    analysis — 4/5 + 2/3 exceeds a whole, so Gehad's objection stands; 7/8 −
    3/4 connect, Hend's 1/8 correct); L3 Adding and Subtracting Fractions with
    Unlike Denominators pp. 7–8 (unlike→like rewriting, estimation
    reasonableness; chamomile crops; "Who Is Correct" on 1/12+2/3 — Soliman's
    9/12 and Samar's 3/4 both correct, Seif's 3/15 wrong); L4 More of Adding and
    Subtracting Fractions with Unlike Denominators pp. 9–10 (including
    three-term expressions).
- **Unit 8 — Adding and Subtracting Mixed Numbers.**
  - Concept 8.1 Working with Mixed Numbers: L1 Adding and Subtracting Mixed
    Numbers with Like Denominators pp. 12–13 (mixed↔improper conversion chart;
    missing-addend match; cotton harvest regrouping); L2 Finding Like
    Denominators of The Mixed Numbers pp. 14–17 (any common denominator
    accepted, with "is there a smaller one?" prompts — e.g. rewriting 16/24 and
    3/5 with 120, then seeking smaller; partner "Choose a Mixed Number" game;
    Egyptian cotton connect).
  - Concept 8.2 Adding and Subtracting Mixed Numbers with Unlike Denominators:
    L3 Using Models to Add and Subtract Mixed Numbers pp. 18–19 (area models,
    number lines; potted-plants error check); L4 Adding and Subtracting Mixed
    Numbers pp. 20–21 (regrouping/borrowing; Wael's dates error — 4 1/4 −
    2 3/5 shown as 2 7/20 is wrong, correct 1 13/20); L5 More of Adding and
    Subtracting Mixed Numbers pp. 22–23 (four strategies compared for
    7 7/9 − 5 8/12, including incorrect ones; missing-number equations); L6
    Story Problems with Mixed Numbers pp. 24–26 (mixed-number time
    conversions; Nile ship trip; thistle planting; juice mixture; "You Be the
    Teacher" — four student solutions for Afaf's hours, including
    time-conversion errors).
- **Unit 9 — Multiplying and Dividing Fractions.**
  - Concept 9.1 Multiplying Fractions and Mixed Numbers: L1 Multiplying a
    Fraction or Mixed Number by a Whole Number pp. 28–29 (repeated addition,
    number line, diagram, unit conversion; garden perimeter; roses in bloom);
    L2 Multiplying Fractions using Models pp. 30–31 (area models for
    fraction×fraction; missing-number models); L3 Multiplying Fractions by
    Fractions pp. 32–33 (numerator×numerator / denominator×denominator,
    simplifying; garden fractions; pea pods connect); L4 Multiplying Fractions
    and Mixed Numbers pp. 34–35 (fraction×mixed via distributive property;
    commutativity partner game; error-find on 3 5/8 × 2/3 — both displayed
    student solutions wrong in different ways); L5 Multiplying Mixed Numbers
    Using Improper Fractions pp. 36–37 (mixed×mixed via improper fractions;
    match game; fertilizer connect — Ayman's 3 1/2 × 7 3/4 claim checked); L6
    Story Problems Involving Multiplication of Fractions and Mixed Numbers
    pp. 38–39 (tomatoes/potatoes "times more", sugarcane, soil bags, reading
    rate; Gamila's "multiplying by 1/2 is like division").
  - Concept 9.2 Dividing Whole Numbers and Unit Fractions: L7 Fractions as
    Division pp. 40–41 (sharing cotton bales; remainders written as fractions;
    3/4 as a division problem); L8 Dividing Unit Fractions by Whole Numbers
    pp. 42–43 (area models; divide-or-multiply inverse pairs, e.g. 1/3÷4 vs
    1/3×1/4); L9 Dividing Whole Numbers by Unit Fractions pp. 44–45 (area
    models; missing factors with 1/3, 1/4; flower-shop connect comparing 7÷1/5
    vs 1/5÷7 direction); L10 Story Problems Involving Division of Whole
    Numbers and Unit Fractions pp. 46–48 (choose-the-operation stories —
    hummus, fava beans, paint, cat food, schoolwork, juice, turtle, pencils,
    gifts, weeding, bread, computer, milk powder, clay figures; explicit 6÷1/4
    vs 1/4÷6 distinction).

**Theme 4 | Applications of Geometry and Measurement.**

- **Unit 10 — Two-Dimensional Figures and Coordinate Planes.**
  - Concept 10.1 Investigating Attributes of Shapes: L1 Categories of Shapes
    pp. 50–53 (classifying 2D figures by attributes; acute/obtuse Venn
    diagrams; quadrilateral hierarchy — rectangle, parallelogram, rhombus,
    square, trapezium, kite; Cairo urban-planning connect); L2 Tricky Triangles
    pp. 54–58 (triangle classification by angles and sides —
    scalene/isosceles/equilateral × acute/right/obtuse; impossible triangles on
    dot paper — no two right or two obtuse angles; tangram art; Mansoura truss
    bridge); L3 Using Tiling to Calculate Area pp. 59–62 (tiling rectangles
    including fractional dimensions 4×2 1/2 and 6 1/2×4 1/2); L4 Applying the
    Area Formula pp. 63–65 (length×width with fractional dimensions; Egyptian
    Museum floor-plan rooms 12 and 17).
  - Concept 10.2 Coordinate Planes: L5 Introduction to Coordinate Planes
    pp. 66–68 (number lines → coordinate plane; x-axis, y-axis, origin; first
    quadrant only, 0–10; Giza pyramids map); L6 Plotting Points on a Coordinate
    Plane pp. 69–72 (ordered pairs (x, y), x-coordinate/y-coordinate vocabulary;
    "Four Points in a Row" game; National Library map); L7 Coordinate Designs
    pp. 73–77 (grid-plan city; plotting polygons; symmetry; challenge figures
    — star, hexagon, house, pentagon, right triangle; Giza Zoo parallelogram
    placement); L8 Representing Points and Creating Patterns pp. 78–82
    (patterns → ordered pairs; Ehab's swapped-coordinate error — pairs
    (2,4),(3,6),(4,8),(5,10),(6,12),(7,14) plotted with x and y reversed;
    garden-border pattern; microbus pattern); L9 Graphing Real-World Data
    pp. 83–86 (rule l=2w; cookie earnings 5 LE/bag; bike race 30 vs 60 km/hr;
    meerkat growth graph; Cairo construction).
- **Unit 11 — Volume.**
  - Concept 11.1 Understanding Volume and Capacity: L1 Geometric Shapes Around
    Us pp. 88–92 (3D solids: cube, cone, cylinder, rectangular prism, sphere,
    square pyramid; faces/edges/vertices table; volume vs capacity;
    pyramid-vs-prism capacity discussion — Seif's claim that a same-base
    same-height pyramid holds more is false); L2 Measuring Volume pp. 93–95
    (volume by counting unit cubes; folding nets into boxes; term "cuboid"
    used for rectangular prism; Saqqara Step Pyramid — 330,400 cubic metres of
    stone, 7×7 central shaft); L3 Same Volume, Different Shape pp. 96–99 (same
    volume, different prisms; painted-cube face counts; layers/slices; Rhind
    Papyrus granary capacity).
  - Concept 11.2 Measuring Volume: L4 Finding a Formula pp. 100–103 (V = base
    area × height, derived from layers; Senet boards — Abdallah's board
    30×8×6=1,440 cm³ vs Doha's board 10×20×6=1,200 cm³: both boards have 30
    squares on top, but their top areas differ (240 vs 200 cm²), so equal
    square count does not mean equal volume); L5 Using a
    Formula to Find Volume pp. 104–107 (V=l×w×h; missing dimension; King's
    Chamber 10.5×5×6 m); L6 Finding the Volume of Compound Shapes pp. 108–110
    (compound cuboids by splitting; Ali's error — adding 18+4+12=34 instead of
    multiplying; the correct volume is 18×12×4 = 864 m³, computed from the
    printed dimensions, not a printed value in the lesson; Karnak
    post-and-lintel model); L7 Solving
    Real-World Volume Story Problems pp. 111–113 (planter boxes, sarcophagus
    fit, shed height, canopic chest 54×49×35 cm; ancient carpenter's chest).
- **Unit 12 — Pie Charts.**
  - Concept 12.1 Understanding Pie Charts: L1 Exploring Pie Charts pp. 115–121
    (graph-type review — line plot, bar, double bar, line graph; pie-chart
    elements; fractions of a circle, decimals, and degrees — circle = 360°;
    sports survey football 50/basketball 25/swimming 15/gymnastics 10 of 100;
    fruit counts); L2 Interpreting data in a Pie Chart pp. 122–125
    (reconstructing an ice-cream key from clues; breakfast choices
    frequency→decimal→fraction; sample-size variability — 10 vs 100 vs 1,000
    citizens surveyed); L3 Making Pie Charts pp. 126–127 (shading pies from
    frequency tables — ice-cream flavours of 50 children; community-building
    pie; gallery walk).

#### Honest negatives (Work 2)

No decimal multiplication/division (that was Term 1); no negative numbers;
coordinate work is first quadrant only; volume is rectangular prisms/cuboids
only (no pyramid/cone volume formulas — pyramids appear only as contexts); pie
charts are read and made from fractions and counts, not from angle measurement
with a protractor (degrees are matched to fractions, not constructed by angle).

#### Held-copy defects and slips (Work 2)

None observed in the English scan; the blank PDF p. 4 is the printed p. iv leaf
(Foreword p. iii → letter p. v), not missing content. Print-table count vs held
file: the English imprint's Arabic print table lists 140 while the held PDF has
136 pages — same 4-page pattern as Work 1, lesson run complete (pp. 3–127 plus
imprint present), difference unexplained from the held copy and recorded, not
explained away.

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages; 33 lessons)

**Theme 1 | Number Sense and Operations: Expressions and Equations.**

- **Unit 1 — Divisibility, Factors, and Multiples.**
  - Concept 1.1 Divisibility, GCF and LCM: L1 Divisibility pp. 1–2 (rules for
    2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10; Food Bank volunteer counts 21, 102, 225, 120, 100, 101);
    L2 Factorizing a number to its prime Factors pp. 3–4 (factor trees; Venn
    diagrams for GCF/LCM of 12 and 18; relatively prime numbers — GCF 1,
    LCM = product); L3 Writing Expressions Using the G.C.F. pp. 5–7 (12 grain
    bags + 8 cheese packs → 4(3+2); 36 + 48 → 12(3+4); distributive property
    stated); L4 Analyzing Least Common Multiples pp. 8–12 (fruit packages —
    cherimoya 2, persimmons 4, bananas 8, dates 16 pieces; partial-package sums
    via LCM denominators). Printed pp. 13–14 carry the Unit 2 opener spread.
- **Unit 2 — Rational Numbers.**
  - Concept 2.1 Explore the Number Line: L1 Using a Number Line to Describe
    Data pp. 15–18 (negatives on the line; Egypt elevations −145 m Western
    Desert to 2,875 m Sinai; freezing points — corn oil −20, fresh water 0,
    seawater −2, peanut oil 3, orange juice −6 °C; biscuit-sharing debt model);
    L2 Using a Number Line and Symbols to Compare Numbers pp. 19–22
    (rock-climber positions −9 to 3; opposites/additive inverse; −(−3);
    true/false set including "zero is its own additive inverse").
  - Concept 2.2 Investigate Rational Numbers: L3 Analyzing Rational Numbers by
    Using Models pp. 23–25 (counting ⊂ natural ⊂ integer ⊂ rational as the
    book presents them; Venn placement; plotting rationals and opposites;
    error analysis on plotted points); L4 Comparing and Ordering Rational
    Numbers pp. 26–27 (least-to-greatest ordering; numbers between pairs, e.g.
    between 3.75 and 3.76).
  - Concept 2.3 Interpret and Use Absolute Value: L5 Exploring Absolute Value
    pp. 28–30 (fish dives/jumps; meeting-time ±5 minutes; token debts — Saleh
    10, Omar −8, Nour −2, Tarek 8; |−2|=2); L6 Comparing Absolute Values
    pp. 31–33 (comparisons with bars; Rawan's debt error — −3.25 > 6.50 claim;
    freezers −17 °C vs −33 °C; pond positional distances).
- **Unit 3 — Algebraic Expressions.**
  - Concept 3.1 Use and Analyze Expressions: L1 Creating Mathematical
    Expressions pp. 35–38 (planetarium/astronaut height +0.05 m in space; Moon
    weight 1/6 of Earth weight; 8 hours sleep per day in space; sorting numeric
    vs algebraic expressions); L2 Analyzing Mathematical Expressions
    pp. 39–42 (terms, like terms, constants, coefficients; arcade tokens
    10x + 20x + 250; Warda/Reda coefficient dispute); L3 Writing Algebraic
    Expressions pp. 43–46 (operation words; verbal ↔ algebraic translation;
    "less than" order switch — "a number less than ten" is 10 − x; Fares's gas
    at 15 km per litre; Aunt Farha's eggs problem).
  - Concept 3.2 Algebraic Expressions and Exponents: L4 Ordering of Operations
    and Exponents pp. 47–49 (feteer takeout; 10², 10³; three-student analysis of
    8 + 2(6 − 2) ÷ 2³; operation order: parentheses, exponents, multiply/divide
    left to right, add/subtract left to right); L5 Evaluating Algebraic
    Expressions pp. 50–51 (T-shirts 100 LE each, 40 LE coupon; evaluating
    6 ÷ (8x − 3) at x = 0.5); L6 Applications on Algebraic Expressions
    pp. 52–54 (stairs worked example; 9 + p² − 3 ÷ 2 for p = 5; 7 + 6t² − 3 for
    t = 4); L7 Determining Equivalent Algebraic Expressions pp. 55–57 (balance
    scale "Keep It Level"; x + 2x vs 2(x + 2) shown NOT equal at x = 1 and 2;
    3(2x + 1) vs 6x + 3; 3x + 6 vs x + 3 + 2(x + 1); 4x + 6 vs 3(x + 2);
    sometimes-equal discussion; 2(x + 1) vs 2x + x).
- **Unit 4 — Equations and Inequalities.**
  - Concept 4.1 Write and Solve Equations and Inequalities: L1 Solving
    Algebraic Equations pp. 59–60 (pan balance models; 3x = 9, x + 8 = 17,
    3 + x = 12, 6x = 18 — one-step equations only); L2 Exploring Inequalities
    pp. 61–63 (everyday restriction signs: speed limit, sale markdown, ride
    height limits, ramp weight limits — stowed 47,000 kg, transiting 24,500
    kg); L3 Solving Inequalities pp. 64–66 (inequalities on number lines;
    solution sets in integers vs rationals distinguished; x > −2 vs x ≥ −2
    comparisons; solutions of x < 9).

**Theme 2 | Mathematical Operations and Algebraic Thinking: Statistics and Data Analysis.**

- **Unit 5 — Dependent and Independent Variables.**
  - Concept 5.1 Explore Relationship between Two Variables: L1 The
    Relationship Between Dependent and Independent Variables pp. 69–71
    (amusement park tickets; dependency phrases; formal definitions); L2
    Applications on Dependent and Independent Variables pp. 72–73 (t = 6r;
    ride ticket table — Ferris Wheel 3, Rock N Roller 6, Scrambler 8, Swing 4,
    Viking 5 tickets; popcorn tokens; a student's reversed-dependency claim
    corrected); L3 Analyzing The Relationship Between Dependent and Independent
    Variables pp. 74–75 (input/output rules; y = 3x; equations from tables;
    "multiply by 3 and add 4" → y = 3x + 4); L4 Graph Representation for
    Dependent and Independent Variables pp. 76–79 (games tables, equations and
    graphs — Ring Toss, Balloon Pop, Basketball; ride equation; independent
    variable on the x-axis).
- **Unit 6 — Data Distributions.**
  - Concept 6.1 Applications on Collecting and Representing Data: L1 Data and
    Statistical Questions pp. 81–84 (time capsule: siblings, plane flights,
    lunches, record players, TV shows; statistical vs non-statistical
    questions; numerical vs categorical data); L2 Exploring the Histogram
    pp. 85–87 (dot plots; bar graphs vs histograms; histogram characteristics
    — grouped intervals, touching bars, no gaps or overlaps); L3 Representing
    Data Using Histograms pp. 88–89 (arm-span frequency data; choosing
    intervals; tree circumferences — Maple 127 cm, Oak 182.9 cm, Dogwood
    91.4 cm, Sassafras 137.2 cm, Ash 148.6 cm; number-cube sums); L4 Exploring
    Box Plot pp. 90–93 (median; five-number summary; quartiles Q1/Q3;
    whiskers; "the box shows where half of the data is located"); L5
    Applications on Data Representations pp. 94–97 (same songs data shown as
    histogram, dot plot and box plot; which questions each display can and
    cannot answer; music-minutes data set; choosing a display for a typical
    value).
- **Unit 7 — Measures of Central Tendency and Spread.**
  - Concept 7.1 Exploring Measures of Central Tendency and Spread: L1 Exploring
    the Balance of Data Sets pp. 99–101 (mean as balance point; class sizes
    22, 19, 27, 25, 29, 21, 25; dot-plot counter shifting); L2 Interpreting Mean
    pp. 102–104 (mean as fair share; greeting cards 32, 34, 36, 38, 40; pencil
    groups; sum ÷ count algorithm); L3 Exploring Median, Mode, and Outliers
    pp. 105–108 (data set 0, 1, 7, 4, 18, 12, 0, 0, 9, 12, 11, 13, 17, 15 → mode
    0, median 10, mean 8.5, outliers none; siblings outlier 9; giraffe heights
    vs exam scores; mean vs median choice under outliers); L4 Exploring The
    Range pp. 109–111 (spring vs summer daily highs: medians both 20 °C,
    ranges 14 vs 10; Omar's points error; band-practice box plot; Nour's quiz
    scores; Running Club vs Hiking Club ages).

#### Honest negatives (Work 3)

No fraction/decimal operations (those are Term 2); no ratio, percent or unit
rate; no coordinate geometry (Term 2); no probability, although the parent
letter names "statistics, and probability" as major work; IQR and MAD appear in
the Unit 7 opener vocabulary but no lesson teaches them — range is the only
spread measure actually taught; equations and inequalities are one-variable and
one-step only (pan-balance models), no multi-step equation solving; no printed
answer key.

#### Held-copy defects and slips (Work 3)

- Front-matter inconsistency (all three languages): the TOC lists the parent
  letter at p. viii, but the letter itself sits on printed pp. vi–vii in
  English, French and Arabic alike. The letter content is complete; only the
  TOC entry is stale. (Checker correction: the Arabic TOC lists the letter
  at p. viii too — verified on the Arabic TOC page, printed p. iv — not
  p. vii as an earlier draft line said.)
- Footer artefact (English text layer, printed p. 13): the Unit 2 opener spread
  (printed pp. 13–14) carries a stray footer line "Lesson 1 • Divisibility 13"
  from the previous lesson. No content is missing — Unit 2 Lesson 1 starts on
  printed p. 15 exactly as the TOC says.
- TOC spelling as printed: "Measures of Centeral Tendency and Spread" [sic]
  for Central.
- Text-layer artefacts in the English extraction (NOT book defects): chart
  fragments bleed into front-matter pages (e.g. a distance-time graphic's
  "Team Two / Top Speed 38 km/hr" text interleaved into TOC/letter pages).
  These appear only in extraction, not on the rendered pages.
- Print-table count vs held file (Arabic): the Arabic back-matter print table
  states 124 pages while the held PDF has 122 pages. The lesson run is complete
  (last lesson plus imprint present); the 2-page difference is unexplained from
  the held copy and is recorded, not explained away.

### WORK 4 — Grade 6, Term 2: fraction and decimal operations, ratio, unit rate and percent, coordinate plane, polygon area, surface area and volume

- Title as printed: English "Math Term 2" (Primary 6); Arabic "الرياضيات /
  الفصل الدراسي الثاني" (Grade 6 pupil book).
- Publisher: Egyptian Ministry of Education and Technical Education; content
  by Discovery Education under the Education 2.0 framework. The parent letter
  names the "Primary 6 Mathematics Techbook" and its ACCESS / BUILD AND
  CONNECT structure, WRAP-UP / PRACTICE / CHECK YOUR UNDERSTANDING features,
  and QR/Quick codes (egm62xx) to the digital Techbook.
- Languages/editions: Arabic 2025-2026 (cover year); English — NO year, ISBN
  or copyright page appears anywhere in the held copy (cover, front matter
  printed pp. ii–iii, TOC, text layer and back imprint printed p. 105 all
  checked; the imprint gives only a Revised-by list, Supervision by Dr. Akram
  Hassan Mohamed, and an Arabic print table: 112 pages, 4 colours, 180 g cover,
  70 g text, 19×27 cm). The English edition's year therefore rests on the
  Arabic parallel (2025-2026) and on the record, NOT on the book itself — see
  record notes below. NO French edition exists in this band (see trap 3).
  English back imprint Revised-by list: Manal Abass Ahmed Azkol, Dr. Mohamed
  Mohyeldin Abdelsalam Aboraia, Dr. Osama Abdelazim Abdelsalam Mohamed,
  Mr. Samir Mohamed Sedawy Mohamed, Ahmed Ibrahim El-Desouky Mohamed Hashim,
  Eman Sayed Ramadan Mohamed Mohamed, George Yuhanna Meikheil Gerges Malaty,
  Sameh Abd El basset El Sayed Mohammed.
- Pagination: English PDF 112 pages; lessons printed pp. 2–104 (Unit 8 opener
  precedes printed p. 2); PDF page = printed page + 7, verified at printed
  pp. 103, 104 and at the imprint (printed p. 105 = PDF p. 112). Front matter:
  cover PDF p. 1; PDF p. 2 printed p. ii; PDF p. 3 printed p. iii; TOC PDF
  pp. 4–5 printed pp. iv–v; parent letter printed pp. vi–vii. Arabic PDF 112
  pages; TOC printed pp. iv–v on PDF pp. 4–5 matches the English TOC exactly
  (its header lists the letter at p. vii where the English TOC lists p. viii —
  a genuine one-page difference from Work 3, where the Arabic TOC lists
  viii; letter itself on vi–vii in both works). The Arabic lesson-page PDF
  offset is +7, checker-pinned at printed pp. 2 (PDF p. 9), 49 (PDF p. 56),
  91 (PDF p. 98) and 104 (PDF p. 111), the same offset as English;
  Arabic lesson folios match English exactly.
- Parallel-edition spot-check evidence: Arabic — full TOC compared
  lesson-for-lesson and page-for-page with English (all 35 lessons, identical
  printed pages). Numbering continues from Term 1: Term 1 = Units 1–7, Term 2
  starts at Unit 8.
- Text-layer caution: the English text layer is noisy in places (OCR-style
  garbling, e.g. a volume passage rendering with broken units, and fraction
  layouts collapsing). Lesson titles, structure and page numbers below come
  from the clean TOC; content summaries come from the text layer, and no
  content claim below rests on a garbled passage alone. End matter (Unit 13
  Lesson 4, imprint) was verified against page renders, not the text layer.

#### Unit-by-unit map (printed pages; 35 lessons)

**Theme 3 | Fractions, Decimals, and Proportional Relationships.**

- **Unit 8 — Operations on Fractions and Decimals.**
  - Concept 8.1 Multiplying and Dividing Fractions and Decimals: L1 Modeling
    Division with fractions and Whole Numbers pp. 2–4 (paper folding: ribbon
    2 ÷ 2/3; string 3/4 ÷ 3; robots 3/4 ÷ 6; paint 2 ÷ 3/5 with a two-student
    error analysis — 3 1/3 vs 3 1/5 containers); L2 Modeling Fraction Division
    with Tape Diagrams pp. 5–7 (bait 1 ÷ 1/6 and 2/3 ÷ 1/6; clay 9/10 ÷ 2/5
    with remainder; trail-mix portion 1/2 ÷ 5/6); L3 Connecting Fraction
    Multiplication to Fraction Division pp. 8–10 (fact families a × b = c,
    c ÷ b = a; "1 of what number is 9" tape diagrams; reciprocal defined by
    inverting; rule test: 1/2 ÷ 4, 1/2 ÷ 3/4, 2/3 ÷ 1/6, checked by
    quotient × divisor = dividend); L4 Analyzing Multiplying and Dividing
    Fractions and Decimals pp. 11–14 (standard algorithms; decimal-point
    placement by fraction reasoning — tenths × tenths → hundredths; apples
    1.5 kg × 40.50 LE with 6.075 / 60.75 / 607.5 distractors; powers of 10 to
    clear decimal divisors; 3.75 ÷ 0.125 conjecture with smallest whole-number
    divisor; 3.66 ÷ 0.3).
- **Unit 9 — Ratio and Its Applications.**
  - Concept 9.1 Understand Ratio: L1 Exploring Ratio and Rate with Real-World
    Situations pp. 16–18 (ratio vs non-ratio comparisons — "seven more than"
    is not a ratio; three forms a:b, a/b, a to b; simplifying by GCF, 6:8 →
    3:4; rate language — per, for every); L2 Representing Ratio pp. 19–21
    (ratios extending patterns; lights on a string, 2 red : 3 blue, extended
    by 5 bulbs; ratio tables).
  - Concept 9.2 Create Equivalent Ratios: L3 Exploring Equivalent Ratios
    pp. 22–23 (rabbits 7 per 2 km²; ratio tables; guava juice 2 kg → 6 cups);
    L4 Representing Ratios with Tape Diagrams pp. 24–25 (wildlife camera
    rabbits : foxes 4 : 1; block value from a known total); L5 Analyzing
    Equivalent Ratios with a Number Line pp. 26–27 (double number lines;
    rabbit speed 6 metres per second; fox 6.5 m/s comparison); L6 Comparing
    and Analyzing Ratios pp. 28–30 (cross-multiplication test; paint mixer —
    6 yellow : 4 red vs 9 yellow : 6 red; Amany's error — adding 2 to each
    term does NOT give an equivalent ratio; ratios equivalent to 8 : 12).
- **Unit 10 — Unit Rate and Percent.**
  - Concept 10.1 Understand Unit Rate: L1 Exploring Unit Rate pp. 32–33 (unit
    rate defined; jogger 6 km/hr, flour for Aish Baladi, cheese, driving
    360 km per 12 litres; unit-rate vs non-unit-rate table); L2 Determining
    Unit Rate pp. 34–35 (jump-rope unit rates by three models — tape diagram,
    double number line, ratio table: Basma 570 jumps / 6 min, Rana 456 / 4,
    Tahani 545 / 5); L3 Using The Unit Rate pp. 36–37 (best buy: popcorn
    small 7 cups / 70 LE, medium 16 / 120, large 20 / 140; lowest cost per cup
    wins).
  - Concept 10.2 Convert Measurements with Ratios: L4 Exploring Conversion
    Factor pp. 38–39 (conversion factor as a ratio of equal amounts in
    different units; Mars Climate Orbiter video; mm/cm statements; factor vs
    non-factor table); L5 Using Conversion Factor pp. 40–41 (Great Pyramid
    14,600 cm → metres; stone block 2,300 kg → grams; camel 20,000 mL → litres;
    caracal 30.5 kg → grams; Sphinx 584 cm → metres); L6 Applications on the
    Conversion Factor pp. 42–44 (multi-step conversions; animal speeds to
    km/h — black mamba 5.6 m/s, coyote 69 km/h, roadrunner 889 cm/s, great
    white shark 0.93 km/min — then ordered slowest to fastest).
  - Concept 10.3 Understand Percent: L7 Exploring Percent pp. 45–47 (100% and
    50% meanings; fraction–decimal–percent equivalents via hundredths and
    10 × 10 grids); L8 Finding the Part, the Whole and the Percent pp. 48–51
    (identifying which value is unknown; zoo browse 800 kg — zebras 60%,
    gorillas 25%, giraffes 15% — via tape diagram, double number line and
    grid); L9 Using Models to Find the Whole pp. 52–54 (40 boxes = 80%;
    80 apples = 16%; 36 pears = 36% with "how many more" follow-up; algorithm
    part ÷ percent); L10 Using Models to Find Percentage pp. 55–58 (Farouk's
    exercise: karate 32/160 = 20%, swimming 18/60 = 30% — swimming higher);
    L11 Applications on Percentage pp. 59–62 (benchmark percents; White
    Friday sales; 10% → 20/30/40/60% by mental math; lunch bill 340 LE with
    5% tax and 15% tip; successive-discount question — a further 15% off after
    40% off; gift budget under 300 LE).

**Theme 4 | Applications of Geometry and Measurement.**

- **Unit 11 — Coordinate Plane.**
  - Concept 11.1 Understand the Coordinate Plane: L1 Exploring the Coordinate
    Plane pp. 64–65 (first-quadrant review; archaeological dig grid; the need
    for other quadrants — labelling (−4, 2) on a first-quadrant-only grid);
    L2 Analyzing The Coordinate Plane pp. 66–68 (four quadrants; sign patterns
    per quadrant; points on axes and the origin; reflection across the x-axis
    and y-axis); L3 Analyzing Points on The Coordinate Plane pp. 69–71
    (rational-number coordinates; zoo map; sting-ray error analysis — Hossam
    (2.5, −6) vs Marwa (−6, 1.5); reflections between enclosures).
  - Concept 11.2 Use Coordinate Geometry: L4 Exploring the Distance between
    Points on a Line pp. 72–73 (distance via absolute value; Fatma's birthday
    party — 5 km of gas; team score spread); L5 Exploring Distance between
    Points on a Coordinate Plane pp. 74–76 (houses map; distances along shared
    x or shared y only; route planning visiting all five houses and returning
    home); L6 Create Geometric Shapes in The Coordinate Plane pp. 77–80
    (identifying square, rectangle, right triangle, trapezium from vertex
    coordinates; right-angle test by coordinates; completing a square from
    (1, 2) with side 3, a rectangle from (−3, −2) with length 6 and width 1).
- **Unit 12 — Area of Some Polygons.**
  - Concept 12.1 Find Area of Parallelogram, Triangle and Trapezium: L1 Area of
    Parallelogram pp. 82–84 (A = b × h; multiple base–height pairs give the
    same area; rhombus as a special parallelogram; square A = s × s, and why
    s × s does NOT work for a rhombus); L2 Area of the Right-Angled Triangle
    pp. 85–87 (skate-ramp faces; A = 1/2 bh; method comparison — which
    student methods are valid; the formula extends to any triangle); L3 Area
    of Acute and Obtuse Triangles pp. 88–89 (heights, including exterior
    heights of obtuse triangles; area unchanged under base–height swaps);
    L4 Exploring Area of Trapezium pp. 90–92 (composing/decomposing;
    worked example totalling 16 1/2 cm²; subtraction method — Afaf's
    rectangle-minus-triangles).
- **Unit 13 — Surface Area and Volume.**
  - Concept 13.1 Use Nets to Find Surface Area: L1 Surface Area of Cuboid
    pp. 94–97 (decomposing a box; net face labelling; cuboid formula
    developed; cube 6s²; Nada's 8 cm sheet-metal box); L2 Exploring Surface
    Area of Prism and Pyramid pp. 98–100 (triangular prism net; square pyramid
    net; tent fabric problem; pyramid of Menkaure — square base side about
    104 m, triangular face height about 84 m).
  - Concept 13.2 Calculate Volume: L3 Applications on Volume pp. 101–102
    (V = b × h or V = l × w × h with fractional sides; concrete mold
    3.5 m × 4.5 m × 2.5 m; three steps 40 cm × 20.25 cm with heights 20.25,
    40.5, 60.75 cm; "at least 24 cubic metres by looking" estimate test); L4
    Volume of Cuboid with Known Ratios pp. 103–104 (doubling 1, 2, then 3
    dimensions of a 20 × 15 × 10 cm lunchbox; volume ratios 2:1, 4:1, 8:1;
    tripling/halving predictions; cooler sized for 24 museum lunches).

#### Honest negatives (Work 4)

No probability (see Work 3 note — the parent letters' "statistics, and
probability" promise is unfulfilled across both Grade 6 terms); no circle area
or circumference; coordinate distance is taught only for points sharing an x
or a y (no diagonal distance, no Pythagoras); percent work covers finding the
part, the whole and the percent, plus discounts, tax and tip, but no formal
percent increase/decrease; no negative-number multiplication or division;
surface area and volume cover cuboids, triangular prisms and square pyramids
only (no spheres, cones or cylinders as taught solids); no printed answer key.

#### Held-copy defects and slips (Work 4)

- Completeness note (English): no copyright page, ISBN or year anywhere in the
  held copy (see editions line above). This is recorded as a property of the
  held copy, not as a claim that the edition lacks them — the scan may simply
  not include a separate copyright leaf. The Arabic parallel carries the year
  (2025-2026) on its cover.
- Text-layer garbling (English extraction only, NOT book defects): fraction
  layouts and some figure labels collapse in extraction (e.g. a volume passage
  rendering with broken units). Page renders were used for end matter; TOC
  values are clean.
- Print-table count vs held file (English): the imprint's Arabic print table
  lists 112 pages and the held PDF has exactly 112 pages — this one matches.
  (Contrast Works 1–3, where the table runs 2–4 pages above the held PDF.)

## Band D — Grades 7–8 (Preparatory 1–2)

Band reader draft. No commits made; this file is the only output besides the
report to the parent orchestrator.

### Band contents

- 8 files, 4 distinct works. Total PDF pages: **961**.
- Grades 7–8 are Egypt's Preparatory stage (First Preparatory = Grade 7,
  Second Preparatory = Grade 8). Each work is a Student's Book in the
  Ministry's 2025–2026 series, framed under Egypt's Vision 2030.
- Publisher for all four works, as printed: the Egyptian Ministry of
  Education and Technical Education. No ISBN is printed in any held copy;
  the colophons carry ministry book numbers instead (listed per work).

### Method and how far each check goes

- Every PDF was verified first: `%PDF` magic, pdfinfo page count, and the
  cover plus title/imprint pages were read from rendered images. No file
  proved to be a different grade, term, language or kind than its record row.
- The two Term 1 works were read IN FULL through their English editions
  (embedded text, checked against rendered covers, printed folios and
  sampled lesson pages). Their Arabic and French editions were verified as
  the same books, not re-read cover to cover: full TOC comparison
  (identical lessons and printed pages), sampled lesson openers at matching
  PDF pages with the same stories and examples, and last pages compared
  question-for-question. This is stated plainly so the parent can judge the
  parallel-edition claim: content once per work, editions spot-checked
  section-for-section, not assumed.
- The two Term 2 works exist in Arabic only and are image-only scans. They
  were read page by page visually, in full, from rendered contact sheets
  (every PDF page viewed, lesson openers, worked examples, assessments,
  unit assessments, activities, final assessments and end matter).
- Printed pages are cited throughout. Pagination was verified against
  printed folios on rendered pages, never assumed:
  - Grade 7 Term 1 (all three languages): PDF page = printed page + 6.
  - Grade 8 Term 1 (all three languages): PDF page = printed page + 5.
  - Grade 7 Term 2 (Arabic): PDF page = printed page + 4.
  - Grade 8 Term 2 (Arabic): PDF page = printed page + 4.
- Lesson anatomy is the same in all four works. Every lesson runs:
  Learning Outcomes / Vocabulary / Previous Skill / Get Ready! story /
  Think & Discuss! / Learn! / worked Examples / Self-Evaluation / Lesson
  Assessment in three parts (First: Measuring Conceptual Understanding,
  multiple choice; Second: Applying Scientific Concepts; Third: Analysis and
  Subjects Integration). Each unit ends with an "Evaluate your
  understanding!" Unit Assessment (tick-box self-rating) and a Unit
  Activity. Each book ends with a Final Assessment in two groups
  (multiple choice, then answer questions). Unit openers carry an
  AI-context paragraph and a life-skills-and-values box.
- Summaries below are the reader's own words. Only very short fragments
  (a heading phrase, a worked-example line) are quoted.

### Work A — Grade 7, Term 1 (Arabic / English / French)

- Title as printed: Arabic الرياضيات (Mathematics); English MATHEMATICS;
  French Mathématiques. Subtitle: First Preparatory – First Term /
  Première Préparatoire – Premier Semestre. Kind: Student's Book
  (كتاب الطالب / Student's Book / Livre de l'élève).
- Publisher: Ministry of Education and Technical Education, Egypt.
  School year as printed: 2025–2026.
- Files: `grade-07_gov_egypt_Math_AR_prp1_TR1.pdf` (121 PDF pages),
  `grade-07_gov_egypt_Mtah_E_Prp1_TR1.pdf` (121),
  `grade-07_gov_egypt_Mtah_F_Prp1_TR1.pdf` (121).
- PDF layout: cover p1; title p2 (English title page doubles as the
  Name/Class/School page); Prepared By p3; contents pp4–5 (printed iv–v);
  Introduction p6; Unit 1 opener p7 = printed 1; content printed 1–113
  (PDF pp7–119); colophon PDF p120; back cover PDF p121.
- Prepared By (English title page): Mr. Osama Zaid Sharif; Prof. Dr. Samar
  Abdel Fattah Lasheen; Ass. Prof. Dr. Amal El-Shahat Hafez; Mr. Magdy
  Abdel Fattah El-Safty; Mr. Ibrahim Abdel Latif El-Saghir; Mr. Bassem
  Magdy Roushdy; Mr. Sameh Labib Attia; Mr. Islam Mohamed Ibrahim; and

- Colophon book numbers as printed: Arabic edition 10/1/11/2/47/207;
  French edition 10/1/11/15/63/1115 (quantity 3000, size 27 × 19 cm,
  70 g white paper, 4 colours, cover 200 g couché, 4 colours). The Arabic
  colophon gives the same size and weights with a 180 g couché cover.
  The English edition's contents footer credits Shorouk Press.
- Parallel-edition evidence: the three contents lists match lesson for
  lesson and page for page; the Arabic last pages match the English
  assessment items question for question (including a rhombus coordinates
  problem); the French sampled lesson openers 1-1, 2-1, 4-1 and 4-6 sit at
  the same PDF pages as the English ones with the same stories (Khaled at
  the Cairo book fair; Salwa at Aswan and Abu Simbel; wind farms; a
  telecom tower). All three editions carry the same robot cover artwork.

#### Unit 1 — Numbers and Their Operations (opener printed 1)

- 1-1 Proportion, printed 2–6. Proportion as equality of two ratios or
  rates; extremes and means; cross multiplication. Get Ready story:
  Khaled at the Cairo International Book Fair, 3 books for 96 LE, buying
  18. Lesson Assessment printed 5–6.
- 1-2 Applications of Ratio and Proportion, printed 7–13. Scale drawings
  (the Iconic Tower in the New Administrative Capital; a map scale of
  1:600,000); proportional division of money, land and weight; percentage
  applications including deduction/discount and sales tax. The lesson
  still carries the older "14% sales tax" phrasing; the rate is outdated
  and should not be taught as current.
- 1-3 Sets and Their Operations, printed 14–18. Listing versus
  set-builder notation; finite, infinite and empty sets; union and
  intersection with Venn diagrams; subsets; union and intersection with
  the empty set. Get Ready: Omar's set of Arab countries whose flags
  contain a crescent moon.
- 1-4 Operations on Integers, printed 19–23. Addition on the number line;
  subtraction as adding the additive inverse; multiplication and division
  of integers and their properties, including which properties subtraction
  and division do and do not share with multiplication. Get Ready: Mount
  Catherine, 2,620 m, South Sinai.
- 1-5 Operations on Rational Numbers, printed 24–29. Forms of a rational
  number including percentage form and calculator conversion; addition
  properties (closure, commutativity and the rest); multiplication and
  division; the multiplicative inverse, noting zero has none. Get Ready:
  Ras Mohamed Nature Reserve. Assessment printed 28–29.
- Unit One Assessment, printed 30.

#### Unit 2 — Algebra (opener printed 31)

- 2-1 Mathematical Expressions and Formulas, printed 32–38. Variables and
  symbols; algebraic term and coefficient; algebraic expression, its terms
  and constant term; expressions versus equations; formulas such as the
  area of a parallelogram; adding and subtracting expressions. Get Ready:
  Salwa's Aswan vacation and Abu Simbel. The temperature formula
  F = 1.8C + 32 is used (a 30 °C conversion exercise).
- 2-2 Linear Equations, printed 39–44. Forming a linear equation in one
  variable (a taxi starting fare of 10 LE in the Get Ready story);
  substitution sets; the addition property (same number added to both
  sides); solving in Q; dividing both sides. Assessment printed 43–44.
- Unit Two Assessment, printed 45. Unit 2 has only these two lessons,
  confirmed in all three language contents lists.

#### Unit 3 — Statistics (opener printed 46)

- 3-1 Organizing Data, printed 47–54. Collecting data by observation,
  interviews and surveys; frequency tables; dot plots; bar graphs;
  histograms with equal interval length; stem-and-leaf plots. Get Ready:
  goals scored in 34 Egyptian League matches.
- 3-2 Arithmetic Mean, printed 55–59. Measures of central tendency; the
  mean of values and of frequency tables; median and mode touched in
  exercises; using Excel for mean, median and mode (printed 58). Get
  Ready: a family's monthly internet consumption in GB.
- 3-3 Pie Charts, printed 60–65. The pie-chart concept; central-angle
  calculation from votes and totals; representing data as pie charts.
  Get Ready: Earth as the "Water/Blue Planet", about 70% water.
- Unit Three Assessment, printed 66–67.

#### Unit 4 — Geometry & Measurement (opener printed 68)

- 4-1 Types of Angles and Relations between Angles, printed 69–76. The
  angle concept and its units; the protractor; zero, acute, right, obtuse,
  straight and reflex angles; adjacent, complementary and supplementary
  angles; angles on a straight line; perpendicularity; angles around a
  point totalling 360°. Get Ready: wind farms and renewable energy.
- 4-2 Parallelism, printed 77–83. Parallel lines; a transversal and its
  eight angles; corresponding, alternate and interior angles; interior
  angles on the same side supplementary; a practical protractor activity;
  "Steps to Write a Proof" (printed 81); proofs of parallelism.
- 4-3 Triangle, printed 84–89. The interior-angle sum via a cut-and-
  rearrange collaborative activity; the exterior angle; the triangle
  inequality (any two sides together exceed the third); classification by
  sides (scalene, isosceles, equilateral).
- 4-4 Quadrilaterals, printed 90–97. The quadrilateral interior sum of
  360°; parallelogram conditions and properties; rectangle, rhombus,
  square and trapezium; a "Quadrilateral Family Tree"; when a
  parallelogram becomes a rectangle, a rhombus or a square.
- 4-5 Polygons, printed 98–103. The polygon concept; convex versus
  concave; the interior-angle sum (n−2)×180°; exterior angles; regular
  pentagon and hexagon work; axes of symmetry, with counts including 0,
  1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 in the exercises. Get Ready: the bees' honeycomb
  polygon pattern.
- 4-6 Coordinates, printed 104–109. Plotting points; quadrants; the
  midpoint formula; projection of a segment on the axes; dividing a
  segment into equal parts, with rhombus and square coordinate problems.
  Assessment printed 108–109.
- Unit Four Assessment, printed 110–111; Unit Four Activity: Tangram,
  starting printed 111.
- Final Assessment, printed 112–113: First Group multiple choice, Second
  Group answer questions.

#### Work A — honest negatives

This book does NOT teach: powers, exponents or roots as a standalone unit
(those are Term 2); inequalities; circle geometry; probability; the
Cartesian product, relations or functions; factorisation; Pythagoras'
theorem; slope or the distance formula (midpoint and projection only);
grouped frequency tables with intervals (those are Grade 8 Term 1);
trigonometry.

  The "26" matches the printed school year 2025–2026.

#### Unit 1 — Numbers and Their Operations (opener printed 1)

- 1-1 Real Numbers, printed 2–7. The irrational number (cannot be written
  a/b with integers a, b, b≠0; set denoted Q′); estimating irrationals;
  the number line; the real numbers R with N⊂Z⊂Q⊂R and a Venn diagram;
  no square root of a negative real number; solving equations in R and in
  Q′ (for example x³−11=5 in R; the worked Q′ example is x²−3=2, and
  the Lesson Assessment item is 2x²−3=9 in Q′). Get Ready: the golden
  rectangle and golden ratio (1+√5)/2, φ, and the Mona Lisa portrait.
  Notes given: √2 ≈ 1.414213562… and ∛5 ≈ 1.709975947…; calculators give
  approximations for infinite non-recurring decimals.
- 1-2 Intervals and Their Operations, printed 8–13. Intervals as subsets
  of R: bounded intervals [a,b], ]a,b[ and half-open forms; unbounded
  intervals including ]−∞,∞[ = R; union, intersection, difference and
  complement of intervals on the number line. Get Ready: Hurghada diving
  and Red Sea tourism.
- 1-3 Operations on Real Numbers, printed 14–19. Addition, subtraction,
  multiplication and division on reals including surd arithmetic; the
  operation properties (closure, commutativity and the rest); additive
  and multiplicative inverses in simplest form. Get Ready: a volcano and
  its magma chamber.
- 1-4 Laws of Square and Cube Roots, printed 20–24. Simplifying to
  simplest form by factorising out perfect squares and cubes (for example
  √32); multiplication and division laws for square and cube roots. Get
  Ready: a school seedbed project.
- 1-5 Laws of Exponents in Real Numbers, printed 25–30. Repeated
  multiplication; multiplication and division laws for powers (same base:
  add or subtract exponents, a≠0); the zero exponent; negative exponents;
  simplifying expressions and then evaluating: worked Example 3 at n=1
  and n=2, Self-Evaluation 3 at n=−2, and the Lesson Assessment at n=1
  and n=3 (the n=0 evaluation sits in the Final Assessment, not in this
  lesson). Get Ready: an educational-toy company.
- Unit One Assessment, printed 31 (a single page). Unit One Activity
  printed 31: "The Golden Triangle".

#### Unit 2 — Algebra: factorisation (opener printed 32)

- 2-1 Factorizing by Taking Out Greatest Common Factor (GCF), printed
  33–38. The GCF method via prime and variable factorisation; factorising
  to find numerical values; solving equations via a×b=0 implying a=0 or
  b=0. Get Ready: Egypt's world-leading squash players.
- 2-2 Factorizing Trinomial, printed 39–45. Trinomials of the forms
  x²+bx+c and ax²+bx+c, using card models, possible-products tables and
  the "scissors" method; verifying solutions by substitution. Get Ready:
  competition in the smartphone industry.
- 2-3 Factorizing Special Cases, printed 46–52. Perfect-square trinomials
  (a±b)²; the difference of two squares; the difference and sum of two
  cubes (including an x³−8 "spot the mistake" exercise); using
  factorisation to evaluate expressions such as 49²−2·49·39+39². Get
  Ready: the base of the Pyramids of Giza.
- 2-4 Factorizing by Grouping, printed 53–59. The grouping method;
  factorising ax²+bx+c (a≠1) by grouping; completing the square (adding
  the square of half the middle-term coefficient, two ways); solving
  quadratics in R by completing the square (for example x²−8x+3=0 and
  x²−2x−4=0). Get Ready: Cairo University Central Library.
- Unit Two Assessment, printed 60 (a single page). Unit Two Activity
  printed 60: Muslim Scientists.

#### Unit 3 — Geometry (opener printed 61)

- 3-1 Congruence, printed 62–69. Congruence in plane shapes; congruent
  polygons (corresponding sides equal and corresponding angles equal,
  vertices named in matching order); triangle congruence cases SSS, SAS,
  ASA and AAS (S = side, A = angle); proofs including parallel conclusions
  and angle-bisector proofs.
- 3-2 Pythagoras' Theorem and Its Converse, printed 70–77. Finding a
  side; the converse; classifying triangles by angles from side lengths
  (acute, right or obtuse by comparing squares). Get Ready: a 13-foot
  ladder against a wall (feet here, centimetres elsewhere in the book).
- 3-3 Applications of Parallelism, printed 78–82. Lengths of segments
  enclosed between parallel lines (the intercept theorem); the midsegment
  theorem (midsegment parallel to the third side and half its length);
  proofs. Get Ready: a road engineer connecting a school and a hospital.
- 3-4 Medians of the Triangle, printed 83–88. The median concept;
  concurrency of the medians; the centroid; the median from the right
  angle equalling half the hypotenuse (with a rectangle-completion proof
  discussed). Get Ready: drones for aerial photography, rescue and
  surveillance.
- 3-5 Isosceles Triangle, printed 89–96. The isosceles triangle theorem
  and its converse; the equilateral triangle and its corollaries
  (equilateral if and only if equiangular); axes of symmetry of a segment
  and of an isosceles triangle (median, bisector and perpendicular
  bisector corollaries); the 30°–60°–90° triangle (the side opposite 30°
  equals half the hypotenuse). Get Ready: Dahab, South Sinai.
- 3-6 Interior and Exterior Angles of Polygons, printed 97–102. Forming
  (n−2) triangles from one vertex; the interior sum (n−2)×180°; exterior
  angles, each pairing with its interior angle to 180°; regular-polygon
  side counts from an interior angle of 150° or an exterior angle of 24°.
  Get Ready: the Octagon building, the new Egyptian Ministry of Defence
  headquarters.
- Unit Three Assessment, printed 103–104. Unit Three Activity printed
  104: a Pythagoras practical.

#### Unit 4 — Statistics (opener printed 105)

- 4-1 Ascending Cumulative Frequency Table and Its Graphical
  Representation, printed 106–111. Grouped frequency tables with
  intervals; ascending cumulative frequency ("less than" upper limits);
  graphical representation; reading counts directly from the table. Get
  Ready: hospital waiting times for 200 patients.
- 4-2 Measurements of Central Tendency of the Frequency Table with
  Intervals, printed 112–118. The estimated mean via interval centres
  (Σf·xm / Σf); the median position and order from the cumulative table;
  mode and quartile context (a Q2 order shown in a worked table); a
  worked equation example solving for k from a mean of 90. Get Ready: a
  student heights table.
- Unit Four Assessment, printed 119–120. Unit Four Activity printed 120:
  a frequency-table data-collection activity.
- Final Assessment, printed 121–122: First Group multiple choice
  (including the multiplicative inverse of −√2 and the item
  ∛2 − 2∛(1/4)),
  Second Group answer questions (intervals X and Y operations; exponent
  simplification evaluated at n=0; a median and midsegment proof with
  DE = 6 cm).

#### Work B — honest negatives

This book does NOT teach: proportion properties or continued proportion
(those are Grade 8 Term 2); the Cartesian product, relations, functions
or linear functions (Grade 8 Term 2); a standalone triangle-inequality
unit (Grade 8 Term 2, lessons 3-1 and 3-2; the inequality itself is in
Grade 7 Term 1, lesson 4-3); circle or solid geometry; sampling or
probability; solving linear equations in one variable as a standalone
lesson (Grade 7 Term 1, lesson 2-2; here equations appear only through
factorisation and completing the square).

- Title as printed: الرياضيات (Mathematics), for the First Preparatory
  grade, Second Term, 2025–2026, Student's Book (كتاب الطالب).
- Publisher: Ministry of Education and Technical Education, Egypt.
  The cover carries the prepared-by panel (the same team as Work A
  minus one name — 8 names; LotfAllah does not appear) and
  the GPS printing-house logo (for printing, publishing and distribution).
- File: `grade-07_gov_egypt_Math_AR_prp1_TR2.pdf` (96 PDF pages,
  image-only apart from a repeated 2025–2026 footer in extraction).
- PDF layout: cover p1; Name/Class/School p2; Introduction p3 (printed
  iii, under Vision 2030, naming the First Preparatory Second Term book);

- Colophon (PDF p96, correct year 2025–2026): reviewed by Manal Abbas
